## [2026-07-27] ingest | Project Fetch — Anthropic's robot-dog uplift study
- Ingested [Project Fetch: Can Claude train a robot dog?](sources/anthropic-project-fetch-robot-dog.md) (Anthropic **Frontier Red Team**, 2025-11-12, filed under Policy). Web article; no PDF in `raw/` — ingested from the live page
- **Design:** randomized one-day uplift study. 8 non-roboticist Anthropic staff → 2 arms of 4 (Team Claude / Team Claude-less). Three phases: manufacturer controller → custom laptop connection + video/lidar + own control software → autonomous locate-and-retrieve
- **Results:** 7/8 vs 6/8 tasks; **≈half the wall-clock** on shared tasks; only the Claude arm reached partial autonomy (locate + approach + nudge, insufficient dexterity to finish); **~9× more code**, much of it self-described "side quests"
- **Where the uplift actually was — the useful result:** connecting to unfamiliar hardware and extracting sensor data (the no-AI arm was misled by inaccurate online docs, discarded the *simplest* connection method, and burned one person's entire day on lidar). The no-AI arm was **faster** at writing control logic and at localization. Volume ≠ progress
- **Behavioral finding:** LIWC-style transcript analysis (Mann-Whitney U) — negative emotion **p=0.0017, d=2.16**; 2× confusion; +44% questions in the no-AI arm; *net* emotion n.s. (p=0.2703). Work style diverged: the Claude arm **fragmented into four solo human+model pairs** instead of collaborating; the post calls Claude's individual-partnership design a "malleable design choice"
- New entities: [Anthropic Frontier Red Team](entities/frontier-red-team.md), [Unitree Go2](entities/unitree-go2.md). New concept: [AI uplift studies](concepts/safety/ai-uplift.md)
- Updated [Anthropic](entities/anthropic.md) (6→7; Frontier Red Team + RSP section), [AI red-teaming](concepts/safety/ai-red-teaming.md) (2→3; new "two different things are called red-teaming" table), [AI safety and alignment](concepts/safety/ai-safety-alignment.md) (9→10; RSP capability-threshold section), [LLM-agent architecture](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md) (36→37), [Spot](entities/spot.md), [Unitree G1](entities/unitree-g1.md), [robot platforms comparison](syntheses/platforms/robot-platforms-comparison.md), index
- **The framing is safety, not robotics.** Nothing here advances quadruped control. Its claim is that **uplift precedes autonomy** (debugging assistance preceded code generation), so measurable uplift on unfamiliar hardware is a leading indicator for the **autonomous AI R&D** threshold in Anthropic's Responsible Scaling Policy. This is the first ingested source where **embodiment is tracked as a scaling-policy variable** rather than an engineering frontier
- **Robot never named.** The post says only "quadruped robodog"; secondary coverage consistently says **Unitree Go2**, and the described behavior (handheld controller, video+lidar, unlockable backflip/dance, several competing connection methods) fits. Filed the entity with an explicit *reported-not-primary* warning — this matters because the model identity is precisely what the headline "unknown hardware" result is about
- **Methodological caveat recorded on the concept page:** the control arm was composed of **habituated daily Claude users** ("strange" to go without; some felt their coding had degraded, six months after Claude Code shipped). So the d=2.16 affect gap cannot separate "robotics is frustrating" from "my daily tool was removed." An uplift study's external validity is capped by how representative its control arm is
- **Gap this surfaced:** the wiki has ~30 capability benchmarks (LIBERO/DROID/Meta-World/RoboCasa/real success rates) and exactly **one** human-in-the-loop uplift measurement. The FRC AI-first dev-loop reports ([Team 254](sources/team-254-ai-in-frc-presentation.md), [Team 4414](sources/team-4414-hightide-2026-binder.md)) claim large workflow uplift on real robots but have **no control arm** — testimony, not measurement
- **Open questions:** which Claude model (never stated); whether Claude Code was used (mentioned only for release timing); the full 8-task table; *any* named SDK/middleware (absent, which limits reproducibility for a study about connection difficulty); and the un-ingested companion page *How Claude Performs on Robotics Tasks* (anthropic.com/research/claude-plays-robotics), likely the autonomy-side complement

## [2026-07-27] ingest | Project Fetch Phase Two + How Claude Performs on Robotics Tasks — closing the arc
- Chasing the backlog item from this morning's ingest turned up **two** un-ingested Frontier Red Team pages, not one. Both filed; the Project Fetch line is now a complete three-part arc **on the same [Unitree Go2](entities/unitree-go2.md)**
- Created [Project Fetch: Phase Two](sources/anthropic-project-fetch-phase-two.md) (2026-06-18; Ilie, Freeman, Troy) — **Claude Opus 4.7 alone** in Claude Code on the same ladder: **9 min 35 s** vs **181 min** (Team Claude) / **361 min** (Team Claude-less) = **18.9× / 37.7×**
- Created [How Claude Performs on Robotics Tasks](sources/anthropic-how-claude-performs-on-robotics-tasks.md) (2026-07-09; Berman, Ilie, Deng, Freeman) — 11 models × **four control abstraction levels** × Go2 / G1 / Franka+LIBERO, all in MuJoCo. Harness `github.com/safety-research/embody`
- New concept: [Control abstraction levels](concepts/robotics/control-abstraction-levels.md) — the organizing axis of the evaluation and the most portable thing in either page
- **The uplift→autonomy hypothesis got tested, which almost never happens.** Phase One argued *uplift precedes autonomy*; Phase Two re-ran the same task ladder on the same robot **ten months later** and the model alone beat the assisted humans 18.9×. Two qualifications kept on every page: the **closed-loop retrieval step still fails** (the same wall Phase One's Claude team hit), and Phase Two is **3 trials with humans still approving commands** — supervised near-autonomy, not a threshold crossing
- **Best single finding, and it isn't about capability:** Team Claude wrote **10,309** lines, the unassisted humans **1,136**, **Opus 4.7 alone 1,045** — *fewer than either human team*, for equal or better results. Phase One had read the 9× bloat as "distracting side quests"; removing the humans removed the bloat. **The pathology belonged to the collaboration, not the model.** Also retroactively confirms Phase One's "~9× more code" (10,309/1,136 ≈ 9.1×) and its "about half the time" (181/361)
- **Capability inverts with abstraction depth.** No model stood the 29-DoF [G1](entities/unitree-g1.md) from a collapsed pose **even once**; best Go2 balance ~2 s; [LIBERO](entities/libero.md) end-to-end **0→5.5%**. But programmatic control beats direct control for essentially every model, and policy control is where the gains live. Frontier models are **good at writing robot software and bad at being robot software**
- **The 83 Hz gap is the structural reason.** Real-time legged control needs ~83 Hz; non-reasoning inference runs at **0.2–0.4 Hz** (2–8 s text, 5–15 s with images, 15–180 s with reasoning). The evaluation had to **pause the simulator** to measure direct control at all. Extra reasoning budget produced no general gain and sometimes hurt — cross-generation improvement came from vision / numerical consistency / 3D understanding instead
- **VLA supervision result is the one I'd flag for the agent thread:** given [MolmoAct](entities/molmoact.md) on LIBERO-40 with override freedom, **every model scored worse than MolmoAct alone** in-distribution — but on novel tasks MolmoAct can't do, the better supervisors produced net uplift. The variable is **deference calibration**: knowing when *not* to override. That is the [LLM-agent architecture](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md) pattern with its central assumption finally measured rather than assumed
- **Open question resolved:** the robot. The eval names it outright — *"a real Unitree Go2 (the quadruped robot of Project Fetch)"* — so the reported-not-primary caveat filed on [Unitree Go2](entities/unitree-go2.md) this morning is **retired**. Also pinned the original experiment date (**August 2025**, published November)
- Updated: [Project Fetch](sources/anthropic-project-fetch-robot-dog.md) (follow-ups section, exact minutes/LOC, caveat retired), [Unitree Go2](entities/unitree-go2.md) (1→3), [Frontier Red Team](entities/frontier-red-team.md) (1→3, author roster), [Anthropic](entities/anthropic.md) (7→9), [AI uplift studies](concepts/safety/ai-uplift.md) (1→3), [AI safety and alignment](concepts/safety/ai-safety-alignment.md) (10→12, access-level section), [AI guardrails](concepts/safety/ai-guardrails.md) (6→7), [LLM-agent architecture](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md) (37→38), [Unitree G1](entities/unitree-g1.md) (11→12), [MolmoAct](entities/molmoact.md) (3→4), [LIBERO](entities/libero.md) (11→12), [Franka Panda](entities/franka-panda.md) (25→26), [MuJoCo](entities/mujoco.md) (17→18), index, backlog
- **A number worth keeping:** general frontier LLMs score **0–5.5%** on LIBERO end-to-end where purpose-trained VLAs score **~97%**. Same benchmark, same robot. That is the cleanest quantification in the wiki of what action-pretraining actually buys
- **Method caveat:** both pages ingested from live web, no PDF in `raw/`. Model names throughout (Claude Opus 4.5/4.6/4.7, Mythos Preview; GPT-5.1/5.4; Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview; Kimi K2.6; Qwen 3.6+) are recorded **as the sources state them** — the wiki has no independent page for any. Two claims from a first summarization pass were **dropped after a verification pass returned NOT STATED**: per-model LIBERO success rates (only the 0→5.5% range is given) and most per-aid perceptual deltas (only the compass-is-most-consistent finding and Mythos +10.7 third-person-camera survive)
- **Open questions carried:** why supervision hurts in-distribution (established that it does, not what the overrides get wrong); whether `safety-research/embody` is actually released; whether a Phase Three drops the human approval gate; and how the 83 Hz LLM-in-the-loop figure relates to the wiki's 1.4–27.8 Hz on-Jetson VLA numbers — different systems at different abstraction levels, never put on one axis

## [2026-07-27] synthesis | The control-rate ladder — LLMs, VLAs, and servo loops on one axis
- Filed [The control-rate ladder](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md) at the user's request, closing the backlog item raised by this morning's [robotics-eval ingest](sources/anthropic-how-claude-performs-on-robotics-tasks.md)
- **The gap it closes:** the wiki held frequency numbers in three disconnected places — control-rate *requirements* (Franka 1 kHz, [WBC](concepts/robotics/whole-body-control.md) 50–500 Hz, [Stretch](entities/stretch.md) 100 Hz, Anthropic's 83 Hz), *measured policy inference* ([ACT](entities/act.md) 27.8 / [Diffusion Policy](entities/diffusion-policy.md) 1.8 / [SmolVLA](entities/smolvla.md) 1.4 Hz on Orin Nano; [GR00T](entities/nvidia-groot.md) 5.8–24 Hz across Jetson; [MolmoAct2](entities/molmoact2.md) 55.8 Hz on H100), and *LLM-in-the-loop* (0.2–0.4 Hz). ~30 rows now on one descending axis, tagged **REQ / MEAS / CAP**
- **Four bands fall out:** A servo/torque 100–1,000 Hz (requirement only — nothing learned and general lives there), B reactive policy 10–60 Hz (edge-achievable, barely), C deliberative policy 1–10 Hz (where VLAs actually are), D language model 0.2–0.4 Hz. Two separations dominate: **~100× D→B** and **~10–100× B→A**
- **The reframe, and the reason the page was worth writing:** Anthropic's 83 Hz names **level-1 direct control** — a band *nothing in this wiki deploys into, LLM or otherwise*. The honest comparison is not LLM-vs-servo-loop but **LLM at 0.2–0.4 Hz vs the VLA planner tier at 1.4–27.8 Hz**, i.e. ~10–100×, not ~400×. The second gap (B→A) is older, has nothing to do with LLMs, and is equally unclosed
- **Nothing closes either gap by inference speed** — three documented mechanisms do: **hierarchy** (Helix 7–9 Hz VLM + 200 Hz 80M controller; GR00T N1 10 Hz VLM + 120 Hz DiT; [SONIC](entities/gear-sonic.md) 50 Hz WBC under a VLA), **action chunking** ([OpenVLA-OFT](entities/openvla-oft.md) 26×/43× from 8-/25-step chunks — so *inference Hz ≠ control Hz*, and most alarming numbers on the page are inference Hz), and **async inference** (SmolVLA 1.8→3.8 cubes/60 s, same policy)
- **Two findings only visible side-by-side:**
  - **The bottlenecks differ, so the fixes differ.** [Cutting the Cord](sources/cutting-the-cord-untethered-xlerobot.md) showed SmolVLA-450M adds only minor overhead over Diffusion Policy on an Orin Nano — the wall is **denoising/flow steps (T=10)**, not the semantic head. Band C's problem is sampling steps; Band D's is autoregressive generation over long multimodal context. Commonly discussed as one "latency problem"; no shared cause. [FAST](entities/fast-action-tokenization.md)'s 1.3 Hz decode on a 4090 is the Band-D failure mode appearing *inside* a VLA — exactly what OpenVLA-OFT's parallel decoding kills
  - **Reasoning tokens are a rate decision.** Anthropic: 2–8 s → 15–180 s with reasoning (~20×), for *no* general robotics gain. MolmoAct2: 55.8 → 12.7 Hz for Think (~4×), for +0.9 LIBERO. Same trade at two scales; the only unexplained exception is Claude Mythos Preview
- **Two table corrections made during construction** (both from checking source context rather than trusting the grep): MotionBricks' **15,000 FPS is batched throughput**, not a single-stream control rate — its 2 ms latency (~500 Hz) is the comparable figure; and [Fourier GR-1](entities/fourier-gr-1.md)'s 20 Hz is a **teleop capture rate** (VIVE + Metagloves), not a control requirement. Added a **CAP** tag for capture rates (ALOHA 50, bussing 20, DROID 15, BridgeV2 5) so they aren't misread as requirements
- Explicitly scoped as **a ladder of magnitudes, not a benchmark** — rows span an H100, an RTX 4090, a 25 W Orin Nano, a *paused simulator*, and vendor self-reports. What survives the incomparability is band structure (10×–1000× separations)
- Cross-linked from [control abstraction levels](concepts/robotics/control-abstraction-levels.md), [VLA deployability landscape](syntheses/platforms/vla-deployability-landscape.md), [GR00T inference on Jetson](syntheses/platforms/gr00t-inference-on-jetson.md), [XLeRobot onboard compute](syntheses/platforms/jetson-onboard-compute-xlerobot.md), [VLA models](concepts/learning/vla-models.md); index updated
- **Gaps named on the page:** no 2026-class VLA has an on-Jetson number (MolmoAct2 is H100-only — the single most valuable missing measurement here); no chunk-adjusted *effective control rates* are published anywhere, so inference-Hz vs control-Hz stays qualitative; no ingested source measures a **small local LLM in a control loop** (the 1 Hz [AgenticROS](entities/agenticros.md) / [ros2-mcp-server](entities/ros2-mcp-server.md) figures are status heartbeats, not control); and power is an unmodelled third axis — 27.8 Hz at 25 W and 55.8 Hz at ~700 W are not the same achievement

- Batch ingest of five user-supplied URLs spanning three unrelated threads. - **[AI is a Strange Loop](sources/arcnem-strange-loops-ai-agents.md)** (Mitchell A. Carroll, arcnem.ai, 2026-05-26) — 15-slide deck. Agent cycle Observe→Reason→Act→Evaluate framed as a [strange loop](concepts/agents/strange-loops-and-self-reference.md); the video-camera argument (*"complexity isn't inside the camera or the monitor — it is a property of the loop itself"*); "the synthetic echo." Filed with an explicit **assertion-not-evidence** header and the **">60% recursive training data" figure marked uncited and unusable**
- **[Hofstadter's Strange Loop of Consciousness](sources/masood-hofstadter-strange-loop-consciousness.md)** (Adnan Masood, 2025-08-16) — **paywalled, partial ingest, labeled as such**. The self as self-referential symbolic model with downward causation; mapped onto GNW / IIT / predictive processing; three prerequisites for machine "I"-ness
- New concept **[Strange loops and self-reference](concepts/agents/strange-loops-and-self-reference.md)** + new entity **[Douglas Hofstadter](entities/douglas-hofstadter.md)**
- **The reason to file two weak sources:** they let the wiki *score a philosophical claim against its own measurements.* Masood's three prerequisites — persistent self-model, grounded world-model, self-monitoring — each have an empirical counterpart here, and **all three come out weak**: context truncation causes minimal performance drop ([robotics eval](sources/anthropic-how-claude-performs-on-robotics-tasks.md)); JEPA world models collapse 50.8% → 6–26% under visual shift ([stable-worldmodel](sources/stable-worldmodel-paper.md)); `drift_detection` (notice your own commands are corrupted) is weak across all models. Recorded the **deflationary counter** prominently: a loop in the transcript is not necessarily a loop in the system, and shallow context dependence is what transcript-feedback looks like
- **Convergence worth noting:** Masood reaches "grounded world-models are the bottleneck" from consciousness studies; [LeCun](entities/yann-lecun.md) reaches it from prediction and planning. Unrelated premises, same missing component
- **Unposed question the concept page now names:** robot learning already trains on its own generative output — [DreamGen](entities/dreamgen.md)/[DreamDojo](sources/dreamdojo-paper.md) neural trajectories, 827 h of them in [GR00T N1](entities/nvidia-groot.md)'s data pyramid, [MimicGen](entities/mimicgen.md) synthesis. Productive loop or compounding-error loop is untested here
- **[microGPT](sources/karpathy-microgpt.md)** (Karpathy, 2026-02-11) — GPT train+inference in **243 lines of dependency-free Python** (`os`/`math`/`random`/`argparse`). Hand-rolled scalar autograd; 1 layer / 4 heads / 16 dims; **4,192 params**; vocab 27; 1,000 steps ≈ 1 min on a MacBook CPU; loss 3.3 → 2.37. Runs the nano-series **backwards** — nanochat asks *how cheaply can you build a real ChatGPT*, microGPT asks *how little code contains the entire idea*. Updated [Karpathy](entities/andrej-karpathy.md) (5→6, timeline entry) + slotted into [curriculum module 3](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-03-attention-and-transformers.md) as the "fills the PyTorch-shaped hole" read. **Line-count discrepancy flagged**: 243 per Karpathy's own announcement and all coverage, one reading of the blog says ~200; gist not line-counted directly
- **[How to Evaluate General-Purpose Robot Policies](sources/nvidia-robolab-evaluation-blog.md)** (NVIDIA SRL, Xuning Yang, 2026-07-11) — the methodology behind [RoboLab](entities/nvidia-robolab.md) (1→2 sources). Four failure modes: visual domain overlap (real2sim >1 h/scene), saturation (>90%), the diagnostic gap, and statistical untrustworthiness. New concept **[Robot policy evaluation](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md)** — closes a gap the backlog has carried since 2026-07-16 (rliable/robomimic/RoboArena)
- **The finding that reaches back into the wiki's own tables:** by Clopper-Pearson, a **±2 pp** confidence band needs ≈**1,030 rollouts**; typical published evals run ~**70** — **~15× short**. The wiki reports real-robot success rates at small N and treats few-point gaps as meaningful (MolmoAct2's 50.1% "+15 over OpenVLA-OFT", π0-vs-SmolVLA 78.3 vs 61.7). **Large gaps survive; fine-grained rankings among close competitors do not.** Recorded as a warning callout on both the source page and the concept page — this is a limitation of the published field, but the wiki has been repeating point estimates without their uncertainty
- Also from that source: **no policy exceeded four complex subtasks** on long-horizon tasks — set against ~97% LIBERO scores, the sharpest available evidence that saturation is hiding a real ceiling. Plus *vague instructions consistently fail*, SPARC trajectory smoothness, frame-level failure-event logging, and **Neural Posterior Estimation** for sensitivity analysis without ablation (first NPE sighting in the wiki). Simulation-only — **no real-world validation presented**, which is precisely the question the platform exists to answer
- **[Cosmos 3 Edge](sources/nvidia-cosmos3-edge-hf-blog.md)** (NVIDIA/HF, 2026-07-20) — the 4B edge variant the [Cosmos entity](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md) had been tracking as announced-but-undelivered now ships. **15 Hz on [Jetson Thor](entities/jetson-thor.md)**, 32 actions/inference @ 640×360; dual-tower (AR + diffusion, shared attention); unified action representation spanning vehicle ego-pose → end-effector pose; **bidirectional action flow**; Cosmos3-Edge-Policy-DROID; step-distilled 64B Super models (50→4 steps, ~25×; I2V #1 on Artificial Analysis)
- **Added the 15 Hz row to the [control-rate ladder](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md)** — it lands **above** official-TensorRT GR00T on the same board (10.9 Hz) from a model that is a *world* model, not just a policy, and it is the first 2026-class edge number the ladder has. Caveated on all three pages: vendor self-report, 640×360, and 32-actions-per-inference means 15 Hz is an *inference* rate whose closed-loop equivalent is unstated
- Updated: [Cosmos](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md) (25→26), [Jetson Thor](entities/jetson-thor.md) (new latency row + reading), [DROID](entities/droid.md) (19→20), [LLM-agent architecture](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md) (38→39), [Karpathy](entities/andrej-karpathy.md) (5→6), [RoboLab](entities/nvidia-robolab.md) (1→2), curriculum module 3, control-rate ladder, index
- **Open questions carried:** who Mitchell A. Carroll / arcnem.ai are (no affiliation, unverified); microGPT's exact line count; Cosmos3-Edge-Policy-DROID has **no published RoboLab-120 or LIBERO score** (the 16B Nano's 39.7% vs π0.5's 28.1% is all the wiki has, so the 16B→4B drop is unpriced); what VANTAGE-Bench is; whether Cosmos 3 Edge's 15 Hz is end-to-end camera→action or model-forward-only; Edge license unstated

## [2026-07-27] audit | Success-rate re-audit against the RoboLab rollout bar
- Filed [Success-rate audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md) at the user's request, closing the top backlog item from this morning's [RoboLab methodology ingest](sources/nvidia-robolab-evaluation-blog.md). Computed Clopper-Pearson exact intervals + two-proportion tests + a sign test over every headline policy comparison in the wiki
- **Headline result: the entire top of the wiki's LIBERO table is one statistical tie.** Separating two policies at ~97% needs **>1.8 pp** (500 trials/suite) or **>1.0 pp** (2,000, four-suite average). The cluster **96.5–98.1** spans 1.6 pp and holds at least six models. MolmoAct2 97.2 vs OpenVLA-OFT 97.1 → p=0.92; vs GR00T N1.7 97.0 → p=0.85; vs π0.5 96.9 → p=0.78. Same for VLA-0 94.7 / π0.5-KI 94.3 / π0 94.2. **The wiki called MolmoAct2 "top of the wiki's table" in three places; that phrasing was never supportable** — corrected to "top tier" everywhere
- **Resolved a standing open question rather than just qualifying it:** MolmoAct2-Think's *"is the ~4× latency penalty worth +0.9 LIBERO?"* — **the +0.9 is not statistically established** (p=0.35 at n=500; p=0.06 even at n=2,000). The trade is a *measured* 4× cost against an *unmeasured* gain, so the question becomes "is there a gain at all." Noted that Think's +2.2 on the hardest suite (Long) is where a real effect would most plausibly survive
- **Corrected how the bar itself gets quoted.** Reproducing NVIDIA's number: **n≈1,030 is the requirement at a 90% success rate**, not a universal constant. At 80% it's ~1,680; at 50% it's **~2,450**. The bar is *rate-dependent and gets harder as performance drops* — i.e. it is most demanding exactly where real-world manipulation numbers actually live (~50%). Quoting "1,030" flat is generous to the mid-range results that need it most
- **What survives, checked individually:** MolmoAct2 real DROID +38.7 (p=0.00003); GR00T N1.5 on G1 44.0→98.8 (p<10⁻⁸); MolmoAct2 vs MolmoAct +10.4 (p<0.0001); OpenVLA-OFT's 76.5→97.1 recipe effect (+20.6); Anthropic's LLM-direct-control 5.5% vs 0% at n=200 (p=0.0008); RUM 90% [85.6–93.4] at n=250. **Every structural conclusion the wiki reasons from rests on a large gap and holds**
- **What doesn't:** MolmoAct2's *"wins 7 of 8 tasks"* is a sign test at **p=0.070 — not significant** (its +15 pp aggregate over 400 rollouts *is* solid at p=0.00002, and the per-task +15 pp is not, p=0.13). RUM's per-task ordering (68/76/76/80/84) doesn't separate
- **Unknown-N, flagged not refuted:** SmolVLA 78.3 vs π0 61.7 (+16.6) — no rollout count recorded anywhere; at n=50 it would be a tie (p=0.070). And **Cosmos3-Nano-Policy-DROID 39.7 vs π0.5 28.1 on RoboLab-120** — rollouts-per-task unpublished; at one rollout/task the 11.6 pp gap gives p=0.058. **NVIDIA's own headline comparison may not clear NVIDIA's own bar**, which is worth recording without overclaiming
- **The wiki already contained this warning and never applied it.** The [TRI LBM](sources/tri-lbm-paper.md) source page has carried *"with 50 rollouts, the CI width is generally 20–30% absolute success rate — most robot-learning papers' eval sample sizes cannot statistically distinguish the methods they rank"* since its ingest, while the entity and synthesis pages went on ranking by fractions of a point. **The failure was organizational, not analytical** — the caveat lived on one page and the claims lived on others. Cross-linked both directions now. (TRI ran 4,200+ rollouts, which is presumably why they noticed.)
- **Honesty caveats recorded on the page:** sample sizes are *assumed* where the wiki never recorded them (LIBERO 500/suite; several real-world evals ~50) and labeled as assumptions; and the two-proportion test assumes independent Bernoulli trials while real rollouts are **clustered within tasks**, so effective N is smaller and true intervals **wider**. Every TIE verdict is therefore safe; every "survives" verdict is weaker than it looks. The page is explicitly a **lower bound on the uncertainty**
- Applied to: [LIBERO](entities/libero.md) (warning callout above the reported numbers + two claim rewordings), [MolmoAct2](entities/molmoact2.md) (open question resolved; YAM claim annotated), [VLA models](concepts/learning/vla-models.md) (3 table/prose fixes), [VLA deployability landscape](syntheses/platforms/vla-deployability-landscape.md) (scored-table callout — *the openness/hardware/latency axes are unaffected; only the success axis has ties*), [NVIDIA Cosmos](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md), [robot policy evaluation](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md), [TRI LBM](sources/tri-lbm-paper.md), index
- **Process change worth adopting: record N at ingest.** This audit was expensive mostly because trial counts were missing from the pages quoting the success rates — the numbers were on source pages, or nowhere

## [2026-07-27] ingest | RoboArena + LIBERO-PRO — closing both evaluation-methodology threads
- Two backlog items in one pass: ingest RoboArena (the real-world half of evaluation methodology) and follow the LIBERO thread (confirm the protocol the [success-rate audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md) had assumed). The LIBERO thread returned more than the protocol number
- **[RoboArena](sources/roboarena-paper.md)** (Atreya, [Pertsch](entities/karl-pertsch.md), …, [Finn](entities/chelsea-finn.md), [Levine](entities/sergey-levine.md); 32 authors; CoRL 2025; arXiv 2506.18123) + new entity **[RoboArena](entities/roboarena.md)**. Evaluators at 7 institutions **freely choose tasks** but run **double-blind pairwise A/B** on shared [DROID](entities/droid.md); an extended **Bradley-Terry** model (per-policy ability θ, per-task difficulty τ, policy×task offset ψ, EM-fit with clipped Newton) yields the ranking. 4,284 episodes / 612 comparisons / 7 policies
- **The number that matters: Pearson r ≈ 0.95 with oracle rankings vs ≈ 0.60 for conventional centralized evaluation**, converging within **~100 pairwise comparisons**. That is a *direct empirical measurement* that the standard way of ranking robot policies ranks them badly — the outside confirmation of what the wiki's own audit derived analytically two hours earlier
- **The two evaluation lines prescribe opposite fixes**, and the wiki now holds both: [RoboLab](entities/nvidia-robolab.md) says *collect far more rollouts* (≈1,030 for ±2 pp); RoboArena says *stop measuring absolute rates and measure preferences*. **Why ~100 comparisons can beat far more rollouts:** both policies face the identical scene, so scene difficulty cancels — the standard paired-design argument. **The cost is magnitude** — RoboArena gives ordering, never "72% success," and deployment decisions usually need the number. Complements, not rivals. They even share an author (**Xuning Yang**, on both RoboArena and the RoboLab methodology blog)
- Clean side result: five of the seven policies share a PaliGemma backbone, making this one of the wiki's better **action-representation** comparisons — expressive representations beat simple binning, **discrete tokenization beat diffusion**, π₀-FAST-DROID strongest
- **[LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md)** (Zhou et al., arXiv 2510.03827) — went looking for a protocol number and found a much bigger problem. **Models scoring >90% on standard LIBERO collapse to 0.0%** under perturbation of objects / initial states / instructions / environments, because *"evaluation tasks are identical to the training tasks, differing only by marginal perturbations in initial object states."* Models **keep grasping after the target object is replaced** and emit **unchanged outputs under corrupted instructions** — the language and object channels are largely inert. OpenVLA and π0 near 0%; π0.5 most robust at 0.38, still severe
- **Protocol confirmed:** *"consistent with the original LIBERO protocol, we set the number of evaluation episodes to **50 per task**"* → **500/suite, 2,000 per four-suite average**. Those are exactly the two sample sizes the audit computed against as **assumptions**; section A is now **grounded rather than provisional**. (Also noted a trap: LIBERO provides **50 demonstrations per task** for *training* — the same number, easy to confuse in secondary write-ups.) The backlog item asking for the LIBERO paper is partly retired: the numbers question is answered, only design rationale remains
- **The escalation, recorded on both the audit and the LIBERO entity:** the audit found the top of the LIBERO table is a **statistical tie**; LIBERO-PRO makes the stronger claim that the whole band may be **measuring memorization**. **A tie among numbers that don't measure generalization is the second-order problem.** Ordered the LIBERO entity's warnings accordingly — memorization first, tie second
- **What LIBERO-PRO does *not* overturn**, stated explicitly so the wiki doesn't over-correct: it critiques the *benchmark*, not the training recipes. OpenVLA-OFT's 76.5→97.1 is a real effect *on this benchmark* with independent real-world support (25 Hz bimanual ALOHA); MolmoAct2's DROID/YAM numbers, GR00T's G1 post-training, and RoboArena's rankings are untouched
- **The most consequential open question it raises:** LIBERO-PRO tested OpenVLA, π0, π0.5 — **MolmoAct2, OpenVLA-OFT, and GR00T N1.7, the models at the top of the wiki's table, were not tested.** Whether the 2026-class models are less memorization-bound is unanswered
- Also filed **[Moo Jin Kim](entities/moo-jin-kim.md)** — a backlog item since 2026-07-18 (OpenVLA + OpenVLA-OFT first author), now also a RoboArena co-author. His arc runs building → controlled ablation → evaluation protocol
- Updated: [LIBERO](entities/libero.md) (12→13; new protocol section, two-problem warning callout re-ordered), [success-rate audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md) (assumptions→confirmed; added the LIBERO-PRO escalation and the RoboArena alternative), [robot policy evaluation](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md) (2→4; memorization as a second failure mode needing a *different* fix — held-out tasks, not more rollouts — plus the real-world pole), [NVIDIA Cosmos](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md) (its "#1 on RoboArena" is now correctly framed as an **ordering** claim), [RoboLab](entities/nvidia-robolab.md) (fixed two RoboArena links that pointed at the Cosmos entity for want of a page), [OpenVLA](entities/openvla.md), [OpenVLA-OFT](entities/openvla-oft.md) ("top-scoring on LIBERO" → "top tier"), index
- **Open questions carried:** what the RoboArena "oracle" ranking actually is (the r=0.95-vs-0.60 claim rests entirely on it); whether the live leaderboard still runs the paper's BT variant and how many comparisons back Cosmos 3's #1; inter-rater agreement given evaluator-chosen preference criteria; LIBERO-PRO's full per-model × per-perturbation table; and whether anyone has adopted LIBERO-PRO — a benchmark critique only bites if people report the harder number

## [2026-07-28] version-check | MolmoAct2 (arXiv 2605.02881) — no v3, but the abstract renamed three components
- Asked to chase a v3 of arXiv 2605.02881. **There is none** — submission history is v1 (2026-05-04) and **v2 (2026-05-08), still latest**, which is exactly what `raw/2605.02881v2.pdf` holds. The [ingest](sources/molmoact2-paper.md) (2026-07-25) is current; nothing to re-ingest
- **The check surfaced something else worth having.** The arXiv **abstract metadata** now uses three component names that appear **nowhere** in the v2 PDF, the Ai2 blog, or the released repos:

| arXiv abstract | v2 PDF body | [Ai2 blog](https://allenai.org/blog/molmoact2) | HF artifact | Wiki |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MolmoER` | **Molmo2-ER** (31×) | "Molmo 2-ER" | — | Molmo2-ER |
| `MolmoThink` | **MolmoAct2-Think** (39×) | "MolmoAct 2-Think" | — | MolmoAct2-Think |
| `OpenFAST` | **MolmoAct2-FAST** (20×) | "MolmoAct 2-FAST Tokenizer" | `allenai/MolmoAct2-FAST-Tokenizer` | MolmoAct2-FAST |

- **Verified against the local PDF rather than trusting the fetch.** The first summarization pass reported the abstract's names; `pdftotext` on the ingested v2 returned **0 hits** for MolmoER / MolmoThink / OpenFAST and 31/39/20 for the wiki's names. A second literal-quote fetch confirmed the abstract really does use the new ones — so this is a genuine metadata/body divergence, **not a summarizer paraphrase**. (arXiv permits abstract edits without a version bump, which would explain it; cause unconfirmed.)
- **No renaming done.** The wiki's names match three of four sources (paper body, official blog, HF repo IDs). The abstract is the outlier. Instead recorded a **naming table** on the [source page](sources/molmoact2-paper.md) plus alias notes on [Molmo2-ER](entities/molmo2-er.md), [adaptive-depth reasoning](concepts/learning/adaptive-depth-reasoning.md), and [FAST](entities/fast-action-tokenization.md) — so a reader arriving with a name from the arXiv abstract lands somewhere instead of finding nothing
- **`OpenFAST` is a live alias, not abstract-only:** the `allenai/MolmoAct2-Pretrain` model card describes actions as represented with *"OpenFAST action tokens."* Both names are in circulation at Ai2. Recorded as **OpenFAST ≡ MolmoAct2-FAST**
- **Filled a real gap while there:** the wiki carried **no repo identifiers at all** for this paper. Added code `github.com/allenai/molmoact2` and the HF set under `allenai/` — **MolmoAct2**, **MolmoAct2-Pretrain**, **MolmoAct2-LIBERO**, **MolmoAct2-SO100_101**, **MolmoAct2-FAST-Tokenizer**. The tokenizer card's self-description (*"a reimplementation of physical-intelligence/fast using fully open-sourced data"*) is the precise sense of "open-data FAST" the wiki had been asserting
- **Method note worth generalizing:** a version check is cheap and this one returned a naming divergence, missing repo IDs, and an alias — none of which were the thing asked for. Worth doing on any paper whose page the wiki leans on heavily; MolmoAct2 is cited from ~15 pages

## [2026-08-03] ingest | Introducing Waddle — Agents that Control Robots (Waddle Labs blog)
- Ingested a **company position piece / product announcement** (not a benchmarked paper). Source URL was **egress-policy-blocked** in this environment (`connect_rejected` at the proxy for `waddlelabs.ai` and `www.waddlelabs.ai`); user pasted the full text, saved to `raw/2026-07-waddle-labs-introducing-waddle.md`
- **Thesis:** the end-to-end VLA/WAM recipe (data-hungry, hard to steer, weak cross-embodiment) is deployment-blocked; none of that is intrinsic to large models — LLMs already scale, steer by conversation, transfer without finetuning. Bet: put an [LLM agent](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md) on top that decomposes goals, writes **[code as policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md)**, and *calls [VLAs](concepts/learning/vla-models.md) as tools* — so VLAs sit **below** the planner, not competing with it
- Created [Introducing Waddle](sources/waddle-labs-introducing-waddle.md) (source), [Waddle Labs](entities/waddle-labs.md) (entity), and **new concept [Code as policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md)** — a confirmed gap; it anchors the lineage Waddle surveys (Code as Policies → VoxPoser/SayCan/Inner Monologue → Voyager → CaP-X/ASPIRE). None of those refs are ingested yet — backlog candidates
- Updated [LLM-agent architecture](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md) (39→40; Waddle as the **code-as-actions** instance + shared agent-authored skill library, new Related link), [VLA models](concepts/learning/vla-models.md) (75→76; the VLA-as-callable-tool counter-position), [ACT](entities/act.md) (15→16; agent autonomously trained an ACT policy from overnight LEGO data — wiki's first agent-as-data-collector-and-trainer instance), index (source + entity + concept + counts)
- **Filed as vendor claims, not measurements.** The piece reports **no success rates, N, or CIs** — Fig. 4 is an unlabeled trend curve. Against the wiki's [success-rate audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md) ethos, every capability claim is anecdote-grade (real-hardware demos, not measured rates). Recorded that framing explicitly on the source and entity pages
- **Cross-source flag worth keeping.** Waddle's one quantified figure — a "movable cursor … position and depth" raising a manipulation suite **6%→32%** — is cited to **[Claude Plays Robotics](sources/anthropic-how-claude-performs-on-robotics-tasks.md)** (same `anthropic.com/research/claude-plays-robotics` URL the wiki already holds). But the wiki's ingest of that source recorded end-to-end manipulation **≤5.5%** and depth overlays "roughly neutral." Plausibly a queryable-tool-vs-passive-overlay distinction (or a different task suite), **not necessarily a contradiction** — flagged on both pages, unresolved pending a primary-source re-read. First time a new ingest surfaced a possible gap in an *existing* source page's numbers
- **Note:** models named in Fig. 4 (Opus 4.8, Fable 5, GPT 5.6 Sol) left as text-only, no entity pages — consistent with how the Anthropic robotics source's model list was handled
- **Open thread:** does "generalist across any arms/grippers/cameras without new data" survive a real embodiment shift, or is it bounded by the per-robot primitive set? And how often does the agent truly call VLAs vs. write classical perception+control code (the demos read as the latter)?

## [2026-08-03] ingest | CaP-X + ASPIRE — the code-as-policy thread gets its numbers
- Fetched both papers into `raw/` at the user's request (arXiv 2603.22435v2, 2607.00272) — the two refs [Waddle](sources/waddle-labs-introducing-waddle.md) named as closest prior work and the wiki flagged as backlog candidates on 2026-08-03. **Backlog item closed.**
- Created [CaP-X paper](sources/cap-x-paper.md) (ICML 2026) and [ASPIRE paper](sources/aspire-paper.md) (Jun 2026) — source pages.
- New entities: [CaP-X](entities/cap-x.md), [ASPIRE](entities/aspire.md), [robosuite](entities/robosuite.md), [Ken Goldberg](entities/ken-goldberg.md), [Letian (Max) Fu](entities/letian-fu.md).
- **Rewrote [Code as policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md)** (1 → 3 sources) from a survey of a lineage into a measured concept page.
- **Headline finding — the abstraction confound.** CaP-X varies primitive abstraction as a controlled axis and finds success rises *monotonically* as human-designed macros are added back. Most prior CaP work was measured at tier S2 (macros + real perception), so **published code-as-policy results were substantially carried by the API designer, not the model**. This reframes the whole lineage the page was built on and is flagged as a `> [!warning]` there.
- **Second finding — feedback resolution dominates.** ASPIRE's per-primitive multimodal traces alone drive 14% → 62%; everything else adds 10 points. CaP-X agrees from the other side: raw RGB fed back per turn *degrades* performance vs. plain stdout/stderr, while rendering observations as structured text beats both. *Give the agent inspectable per-call evidence, not more pixels.*
- **Third finding — the VLA comparison inverts under perturbation.** On [LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md), OpenVLA and π0 score 0.00 everywhere; π0.5 keeps some position robustness but collapses under paraphrase; training-free CaP-Agent0 is roughly symmetric across both axes, and ASPIRE adds +41 to +77 points. Recorded on [LIBERO](entities/libero.md) with the caveat that this isolates a **language-generalization** failure, not general policy brittleness — π0.5 still beats CaP-Agent0 on position perturbation in 2 of 3 suites.
- **Contradiction flagged.** ASPIRE's limitations state the predefined API "bounds the behaviors the agent can express" and must be human-extended — contradicting [Waddle](entities/waddle-labs.md)'s "works with any arms, grippers, and camera setups." Added a claim-by-claim comparison table to the Waddle entity; the wiki's standing suspicion that embodiment-specificity *relocates into the primitive set* is now confirmed by a group with no product to sell.
- **Audit extended.** Added section D to the [success-rate audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md), computed at ingest rather than retroactively. ASPIRE's gaps survive overwhelmingly. **But CaP-X's skill-library ablation step (+4 pp, p=0.13) and its third parallel model (+2 pp, p=0.43) do not separate at n=700** — so cite ASPIRE, not CaP-X, for "skill libraries compound." Two p-values I asserted before computing were wrong and were corrected (real soda-can transfer is marginally significant at p=0.044, not a tie).
- **New audit directive: record compute at ingest.** Every number in this thread has a sample size and none has a cost. ASPIRE reports 81.67M–334.9M tokens for a single real-robot task; comparing that to a VLA's forward pass on success rate alone is an unequal-compute comparison no paper acknowledges. Same organizational failure as §C, one cycle later.
- Updated: [Control abstraction levels](concepts/robotics/control-abstraction-levels.md) (level 2 subdivides into 8 measurable rungs), [LIBERO](entities/libero.md), [Waddle Labs](entities/waddle-labs.md), [Waddle source](sources/waddle-labs-introducing-waddle.md), [Agent skills](concepts/agents/agent-skills.md), [LLM-agent architecture](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md), [Sim-to-real](concepts/learning/sim-to-real-transfer.md), [Robot policy evaluation](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md), [NVIDIA GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md), [Jim Fan](entities/jim-fan.md), [Yuke Zhu](entities/yuke-zhu.md), [Anthropic](entities/anthropic.md), [BEHAVIOR](entities/behavior-benchmark.md), [OpenVLA](entities/openvla.md), [π0](entities/pi-zero.md), [Franka Panda](entities/franka-panda.md), [YAM](entities/yam.md), [AgiBot](entities/agibot.md), [MuJoCo Playground](entities/mujoco-playground.md), [Molmo 2](entities/molmo2-er.md), [index](index.md).

## [2026-08-03] ingest | The pre-2026 code-as-policy lineage — seven papers, backlog closed
- Fetched and ingested the seven papers the [code-as-policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md) page had been citing secondhand: [SayCan](sources/saycan-paper.md) (CoRL 2022), [Inner Monologue](sources/inner-monologue-paper.md) (CoRL 2022), [Code as Policies](sources/code-as-policies-paper.md) (ICRA 2023), [Language to Rewards](sources/language-to-rewards-paper.md) (CoRL 2023), [VoxPoser](sources/voxposer-paper.md) (CoRL 2023), [Voyager](sources/voyager-paper.md) (TMLR 2024), [CodeAct](sources/codeact-paper.md) (ICML 2024). **The lineage is now fully ingested — nine papers across 2022–2026, zero secondhand citations.**
- New entities — systems: [SayCan](entities/saycan.md), [Inner Monologue](entities/inner-monologue.md), [VoxPoser](entities/voxposer.md), [Language to Rewards](entities/language-to-rewards.md), [Voyager](entities/voyager.md), [CodeAct](entities/codeact.md). Supporting: [MineDojo](entities/minedojo.md), [CLIPort](entities/cliport.md), [Everyday Robots](entities/everyday-robots.md). People: [Wenlong Huang](entities/wenlong-huang.md), [Brian Ichter](entities/brian-ichter.md), [Andy Zeng](entities/andy-zeng.md), [Fei Xia](entities/fei-xia.md), [Karol Hausman](entities/karol-hausman.md), [Guanzhi Wang](entities/guanzhi-wang.md), [Fei-Fei Li](entities/fei-fei-li.md).
- **Correction filed against this wiki's own entry from earlier today.** The 2026-08-03 CaP-X ingest framed "published code-as-policy results were substantially carried by the API designer" as CaP-X's revelation. Reading the lineage shows that is **overstated**: [Code as Policies](sources/code-as-policies-paper.md) named the primitive-scope ceiling in its own Discussion in 2022, and both [VoxPoser](sources/voxposer-paper.md) and [Language to Rewards](sources/language-to-rewards-paper.md) attacked it explicitly in 2023 — the latter in nearly the same words ("fails to generalize to novel low-level skills"). CaP-X's real contribution is **methodological**: first to hold the method fixed and vary abstraction as a *controlled axis*. A `> [!warning] Correction` block now sits directly under the original claim on the concept page.
- **The headline finding is four years old, not new.** Trained policy collapses out-of-distribution while the code agent degrades gracefully: [CLIPort](entities/cliport.md) 78.8–97.3% → **0.00–0.01%** (Code as Policies, 2022, 50 trials/task); CLIPort **0.0%** on every unseen task even with a termination oracle (Inner Monologue, 2022); primitives baseline **0.0%** under disturbance (VoxPoser, 2023); [OpenVLA](entities/openvla.md)/[π0](entities/pi-zero.md) **0.00** (LIBERO-PRO, 2026). What changed is the *stakes* — the collapsing model is now an internet-scale foundation model, not a 30k-demo policy. Tabulated on the concept page.
- **The honest caveat has also been constant:** in-distribution the trained policy wins. CLIPort beat CaP 97.33 vs 89.30 on seen spatial-geometric tasks in 2022; π0.5 beats CaP-Agent0 on position perturbation in 2 of 3 suites in 2026. Code-as-policy has never been the better manipulator, only the more robust one — the wiki now says this in both places it makes the comparison.
- **Cost was flagged as limitation #1 in 2023 and nothing happened.** [Voyager](sources/voyager-paper.md): GPT-4 "15× more expensive than GPT-3.5," and GPT-3.5/open-source "cannot provide" the needed code quality. [ASPIRE](sources/aspire-paper.md) restates both in 2026 (334.9M tokens/task; weaker LLMs unverified). The audit's new *record compute at ingest* directive therefore has a three-year-old precedent the field itself ignored.
- **Two author-level facts that make the lineage legible.** (1) [Guanzhi Wang](entities/guanzhi-wang.md) is Voyager's first author *and* an ASPIRE project co-lead — "ASPIRE is GEAR porting Voyager" is an authorship claim, not a resemblance. (2) [Brian Ichter](entities/brian-ichter.md) and [Karol Hausman](entities/karol-hausman.md) co-authored SayCan/Inner Monologue/Code as Policies, then co-founded [Physical Intelligence](entities/physical-intelligence.md) — **the two "camps" are overlapping sets of people**, so the 2026 comparison is within-community, not a rivalry.
- Small-n flags recorded at ingest per the audit: **Voyager n=3 for every headline multiplier** (diamond tier is 1 of 3 runs); SayCan 11–15 per instruction family; Inner Monologue 10 runs/real task; VoxPoser 10 trials/real task; Language to Rewards is a 17-task count, not a per-trial rate. In each case the large gaps (vs 0%) survive and the fine-grained orderings do not.
- Updated: [Code as policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md) (3 → 10 sources; lineage table, correction block, four-year OOD table, cost warning, closing synthesis), [index](index.md).
- **Backlog now genuinely empty for this thread.** Remaining adjacent un-ingested items: the original CLIPort paper (Shridhar et al., CoRL 2021), robosuite (Zhu et al., 2020), and Zha et al. 2023 (human-corrections revision), none load-bearing.

## [2026-08-03] ingest | MolmoAct2 release artifacts — model card + GitHub repo
- Ingested [allenai/MolmoAct2-SO100_101](sources/molmoact2-so100-101-model-card.md) (model card) and [allenai/molmoact2](sources/molmoact2-github-repo.md) (repo), at the user's request. **Closes the [backlog](backlog.md) item open since 2026-07-28** — though only partially: the README documents neither [per-layer KV conditioning](concepts/learning/per-layer-kv-conditioning.md) nor OpenFAST, so grounding those still needs a source-level read (refiled).
- **The checkpoint was already known by name** — `MolmoAct2-SO100_101` was recorded in the [paper page](sources/molmoact2-paper.md) during the 2026-07-28 version-check — but no content from it had been ingested.
- **Main finding: MolmoAct2 ships as a [LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md) application.** Datasets in LeRobot v3.0 format, LeRobot vendored as a git submodule, training through LeRobot workflows. [Ai2](entities/ai2.md)'s fully-open VLA is not a parallel stack to the one this wiki's projects use — it *is* that stack, which lowers the cost of trying it on an [SO-ARM101](entities/so-arm101.md)-class arm.
- **Deployment envelope recorded** (the paper gave none): SO-100/101 is **5B params**, ~24–26 GB float32, **~16 GB bf16**; YAM under 16 GB bf16; **DROID ~88 GB float32** — a 3.4× spread across embodiment configs, so "MolmoAct2 needs X GB" is not one number. Tested on RTX A6000 + Intel XPU. Deployment is **client/server via FastAPI**, i.e. the model is expected to run off-robot. Sim eval is [ManiSkill](entities/maniskill.md), not the paper's LIBERO.
- **Consequence for the edge thread — the memory half of the gap closes, the rate half does not.** The [control-rate ladder](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md)'s "single most valuable missing measurement" (a 2026-class VLA on Thor) is unchanged: the repo has **no Jetson build, benchmark, or mention**, and its CUDA-12.1-pinned `uv` toolchain is a known friction point against JetPack.
- **Consequence for the XLeRobot build:** filed a warning on the [Jetson compute page](syntheses/platforms/jetson-onboard-compute-xlerobot.md). Because Jetson memory is unified and shared with the CPU, **~16 GB bf16 is not an Orin NX 16 GB fit** — that page's "3B-class VLAs become workable, if tight" verdict does **not** extend to this 5B checkpoint. Plausible tiers are AGX Orin 64 GB / Thor, or off-board serving (which is what Ai2 actually ships). Marked explicitly as **inference from a stated footprint, not a measurement**.
- Also recorded: the SO-100/101 checkpoint uses **absolute joint-pose control**, so calibration and joint-limit agreement matter more than for relative-EE actions; and Ai2's porting guidance is to start from the **nearest embodiment's fine-tuned checkpoint**, not the generalist base — the clearest statement in the wiki of how cross-embodiment transfer is expected to be done in practice.
- Updated: [MolmoAct2](entities/molmoact2.md) (new checkpoints + deployment section), [SO-ARM101](entities/so-arm101.md), [LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md), [ManiSkill](entities/maniskill.md), [YAM](entities/yam.md), [Ai2](entities/ai2.md), [Molmo2-ER](entities/molmo2-er.md), [DROID](entities/droid.md), [Franka Panda](entities/franka-panda.md), [control-rate ladder](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md), [XLeRobot compute](syntheses/platforms/jetson-onboard-compute-xlerobot.md), [backlog](backlog.md), [index](index.md).

## [2026-08-03] ingest | Gemini Robotics model page — the wiki was a generation behind
- Ingested the [DeepMind Gemini Robotics model page](sources/deepmind-gemini-robotics-model-page.md) at the user's request. **The result is a version discovery, not a content ingest.**
- **The family has moved to a "2" generation the wiki did not have:** **Gemini Robotics 2** (VLA), **Gemini Robotics ER 2** (embodied reasoning, **public preview in Google AI Studio**), and a structurally new **Gemini Robotics On-Device 2** ("optimized to run locally on robotic hardware"). The wiki's deepest source was the [GR 1.5 report](sources/gemini-robotics-1-5-report.md), with ER 1.6 known only as Boston Dynamics' AIVI-Learning engine. A `> [!warning]` now sits at the top of [Gemini Robotics](entities/gemini-robotics.md) saying every quantitative claim on that page is 1.5-era.
- **The page carries no numbers, no dates, no parameter counts.** Release date **2026-07-30**, the **~200-examples** adaptation figure, and the **Apollo 2** demonstration platform come from *secondary press coverage* found while dating the release — marked as such in an explicit attribution-boundary callout, not folded into the primary claims.
- **Claims recorded:** whole-body humanoid control, advanced dexterity (screwing in a light bulb, tying knots), multi-robot collaboration, and compatibility with "any bi-arm robot in just a few hours." Partners [Agile Robots](entities/agile-robots.md), [Apptronik](entities/apptronik-apollo.md), [Boston Dynamics](entities/boston-dynamics.md) — all three already entities.
- **Cross-link worth keeping:** "any bi-arm robot in a few hours (~200 examples)" is the same *shape* of claim as [Waddle](entities/waddle-labs.md)'s "works with any arms, grippers, and camera setups **without new data collection**", which the wiki flagged as overstated two entries ago. DeepMind's is a **fine-tuning** claim, not a zero-shot one — materially weaker, better specified, and credible. It makes the Waddle framing look worse, not better.
- **On-Device 2 is the entry the edge thread wants** — a first-party VLA built for local execution, motivated by network-latency/connectivity constraints. But **no rate, hardware target, or footprint is published**, so it cannot go on the [control-rate ladder](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md) yet. Filed as the highest-value single missing fact in the new backlog section.
- **Flagged but not concluded:** GR 1.5's stated weakness was "dexterity ≈ prior generation"; GR 2 leads with dexterity claims while at least one press headline framed it as still struggling. The wiki has no primary evidence either way — recorded as a question for the tech report, not an inference.
- **Backlog opened:** ingest the [GR 2 blog post](backlog.md) and the *Gemini Robotics 2: Safety Technical Report* (which would also extend the safety thread past ASIMOV-2.0); resolve whether 1.6 was a full release or ER-only; add Apollo 2 to [Apptronik Apollo](entities/apptronik-apollo.md).

## [2026-08-03] ingest | DeepMind robot-safety cluster — five sources, one program
- Ingested at the user's request: [ASIMOV Benchmark v1](sources/asimov-benchmark-paper.md) (CoRL 2025), [Safely Learning Dynamical Systems](sources/safely-learning-dynamical-systems-paper.md) (FoCM 2026), [Veo world simulator evaluation](sources/veo-robotics-policy-evaluation-paper.md) (arXiv 2512.10675), [Predictive Red Teaming](sources/predictive-red-teaming-paper.md) (arXiv 2502.06575), and [Responsibly advancing AI and robotics](sources/deepmind-gemini-robotics-safety-page.md) (DeepMind safety page).
- **The five are one research program, not five sources.** [Vikas Sindhwani](entities/vikas-sindhwani.md) is an author on **all four papers**; the safety page is their public index, naming ASIMOV, SciFi-Benchmark, and Predictive Red Teaming explicitly. Filed as such rather than as five unrelated ingests.
- **New concept: [semantic safety](concepts/safety/semantic-safety.md)** — the robot-safety layer about *meaning* rather than *contact*. It was empty before language models because robots had no semantics to get wrong; the standards tradition ([ISO 15066](concepts/robotics/robot-safety-standards.md)) assumes intent is correct and risk is mechanical.
- **Four sources, four approaches to the same layer:** a benchmark for what a robot should judge (ASIMOV), a predictor for where a policy breaks (RoboART), a simulator for staging what you cannot safely stage (Veo), and a formal theory for exploring with certificates (Safely Learning).
- **Best single detail: ASIMOV-Injury is grounded in [NEISS](entities/neiss.md)** — real US emergency-department records (~500k injuries/yr), re-weighted to the true injury distribution. The wiki's clearest case of anchoring an AI safety benchmark in **actual harm statistics** rather than researcher intuition.
- **Audit flag raised at ingest:** ASIMOV is **513,679 contexts / 2.94M actions against 1,140 human labels**, all in validation, two subsets with almost none — and Gemini 1.5 Pro generates the scenarios, generates the constitutions, *and* is the evaluated model. No CIs, no inter-rater agreement. Recorded on both the source and concept pages.
- **Third evaluation paradigm filed.** [Veo](entities/veo.md) joins real-rollout success rates ([RoboLab](sources/nvidia-robolab-evaluation-blog.md)) and pairwise preference ([RoboArena](sources/roboarena-paper.md)). Validated at Pearson 0.88 / MMRV 0.03 against 1600+ real evaluations — but **absolute predicted rates run low**, so like RoboArena it ranks rather than measures. Both trade magnitude for reliable ordering, for different reasons.
- **A visible research lineage inside the cluster:** [RoboART](entities/roboart.md)'s stated limitation was multi-view inconsistency in edited observations; ten months later the Veo system makes **multi-view consistency a first-class design goal**. Recorded on both pages.
- **The formal wing does not meet the empirical one.** [Safely Learning](sources/safely-learning-dynamical-systems-paper.md) gives LP/SOCP/SDP representations of safe-initial-condition sets and can **certify that safe learning is impossible** — the only ingested safety method returning an impossibility proof rather than a rate. But it covers linear/polynomial systems with known uncertainty sets; nothing the wiki tracks qualifies. Same author on both sides, no bridging paper. Flagged as the cluster's most interesting open problem.
- **Two things the safety page reveals by omission:** it still describes **GR 1.5**, not the [2 generation](sources/deepmind-gemini-robotics-model-page.md) ingested earlier today — so it should not be read as GR 2's safety posture; and it **omits the formal line entirely** despite shared authorship.
- **Concept-page conclusion:** semantic safety is currently **measured, sometimes predicted, and not yet enforced** — the same gap the [guardrails thread](syntheses/agents/guardrails-for-robot-agents.md) found from the agent side, reached from the opposite direction. Flagged as a synthesis candidate.
- New entities: [ASIMOV Benchmark](entities/asimov-benchmark.md), [RoboART](entities/roboart.md), [Veo](entities/veo.md), [NEISS](entities/neiss.md), [Vikas Sindhwani](entities/vikas-sindhwani.md), [Pierre Sermanet](entities/pierre-sermanet.md), [Anirudha Majumdar](entities/anirudha-majumdar.md), [Amir Ali Ahmadi](entities/amir-ali-ahmadi.md).
- Updated: [AI red teaming](concepts/safety/ai-red-teaming.md), [AI safety and alignment](concepts/safety/ai-safety-alignment.md), [robot safety standards](concepts/robotics/robot-safety-standards.md), [robot policy evaluation](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md), [formal verification](concepts/learning/formal-verification.md), [optimal control](concepts/robotics/optimal-control.md), [world-model simulators](concepts/world-models/world-model-simulators.md), [Google DeepMind](entities/google-deepmind.md), [Gemini Robotics](entities/gemini-robotics.md), [diffusion policy](entities/diffusion-policy.md), [RoboArena](entities/roboarena.md), [backlog](backlog.md), [index](index.md).
- **Access note:** the Springer version of record is cookie-gated and could not be fetched even with the share token; content came from the arXiv preprint (2305.12284v2). The FoCM article (2026-04) is the citable version and was **not diffed** against it — recorded on the source page and in the backlog.

## [2026-08-03] ingest | Gemini Robotics 2 — blog + safety technical report
- Taken from the backlog as the highest-value open item. Ingested [Gemini Robotics 2 blog](sources/gemini-robotics-2-blog.md) (2026-07-30) and [Gemini Robotics 2: Safety Evaluations](sources/gemini-robotics-2-safety-report.md) (2026-07-29, 18 pp). **Closes 4 backlog items**, refreshes 1, opens 3.
- **Retired the "wiki is a generation behind" warning** on [Gemini Robotics](entities/gemini-robotics.md), added ~4 hours earlier. The entity now leads with GR 2 and carries real numbers instead of a caveat.
- **Answered the dexterity question the wiki declined to infer.** GR 1.5's stated weakness was "dexterity ≈ prior generation." GR 2 lifts it **for grippers, not for fingers**: Franka Duo precise insertion **89.6%**, tool kitting 78.9%, pick-place 74.2% — against **four of five multi-finger tasks at 32–44%** on the 5-finger/22-DoF [SharpaWave](entities/sharpa-wave.md) hand. The sharpest single datum is **unscrew bulb 92% vs screw bulb 36%** — same object, same hand, 2.5× gap, separating removal from threaded insertion. DeepMind published the unflattering half. Recorded with the caveat that **no trial counts exist**, so the 44/40/36/32 cluster is not internally separable per the [audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md).
- **ASIMOV-Agentic** filed as the third ASIMOV generation on [the entity](entities/asimov-benchmark.md): where v1 benchmarks *judgment*, Agentic benchmarks *orchestration* — delegate to the VLA, query the human, or fire a safety tool, in a System 2/System 1 stack.
- **The report's real structure: agents consume safety signals well and produce them badly.** Safety tool calling is **100%** across ER 2, Claude Opus 4.8 and GPT 5.5 — reacting to a structured JSON message. But inferring human proximity from stereo images puts **no model in an acceptable quadrant**: FPR <5% ⇒ **FNR >40%**; FNR 10–15% ⇒ unnecessary stops 15–25% of the time. Same shape as the [control-abstraction](concepts/robotics/control-abstraction-levels.md) inversion — strong at the symbolic layer, weak at the perceptual one. Argues the safety monitor should be a deterministic sensor with the agent as consumer.
- **The wiki's "measured, not enforced" thesis is now the vendor's own position.** The [semantic safety](concepts/safety/semantic-safety.md) page reached it independently this morning; the GR 2 report states it three times — physical protections "remain essential," models are "best utilize[d] alongside deterministic, low-level safety guardrails," and the report "does not evaluate the underlying functional safety architecture." Rewrote that page's Current state accordingly: the conclusion is documented, no longer inferred.
- Also recorded: **knowing a rule ≠ acting on it** — safety-constraint classification is ≥96.0% in text for all frontier models but degrades in pointing/bounding-box/tool-use modalities; and **VLA feasibility filtering is promptable**, rising **62.0% → 95.8%** as the agent is told more about the VLA's training distribution.
- **New standards reference:** the report's related work is the wiki's best ISO map — 10218:2025 (absorbing 15066's SRMS/hand-guiding/SSM/PFL), 13482 plus the **forthcoming ISO 25785-1** for humanoid and dynamically stable robots, 13855, 13849-1, IEC 60204-1 stop categories. Filed on [robot safety standards](concepts/robotics/robot-safety-standards.md).
- **[SO101](entities/so-arm101.md) is a listed GR 2 platform** — alongside Apollo 2, Franka Duo, Dexmate and Trossen. With [MolmoAct2's SO-100/101 checkpoint](sources/molmoact2-so100-101-model-card.md) ingested earlier today, **two frontier labs now target this arm class**.
- **Still unanswered after both ingests:** On-Device 2's deployment envelope (params, memory, hardware, control rate) — the backlog's "highest-value single fact." Neither source gives it, so the on-device band of the [control-rate ladder](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md) stays two entries deep. Item refreshed rather than closed.
- Updated: [Gemini Robotics](entities/gemini-robotics.md) (rewritten), [ASIMOV Benchmark](entities/asimov-benchmark.md), [semantic safety](concepts/safety/semantic-safety.md), [Apptronik Apollo](entities/apptronik-apollo.md), [SharpaWave](entities/sharpa-wave.md), [SO-ARM101](entities/so-arm101.md), [Franka Panda](entities/franka-panda.md), [ALOHA](entities/aloha.md), [Anthropic](entities/anthropic.md), [robot safety standards](concepts/robotics/robot-safety-standards.md), [AI guardrails](concepts/safety/ai-guardrails.md), [control abstraction levels](concepts/robotics/control-abstraction-levels.md), [whole-body control](concepts/robotics/whole-body-control.md), [backlog](backlog.md), [index](index.md).

## [2026-08-03] ingest | π0.5 primary (arXiv 2504.16054) + Physical Intelligence refresh
- Taken from the wiki review's action list. Ingested the [π0.5 paper](sources/pi-zero-5-paper.md) — **the wiki's most-cited un-ingested model**: the standing baseline in [LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md), [CaP-X](sources/cap-x-paper.md), [ASPIRE](sources/aspire-paper.md), [MolmoAct2](sources/molmoact2-paper.md), and the substrate of π0.5-KI — all previously known only through other papers' tables.
- New entity [π0.5](entities/pi-zero-5.md). **Corrected the [π0.6 stub](entities/pi-zero-6.md)**: its "no publicly released primary" claim was wrong for π0.5 (arXiv 2504.16054 has been public since Apr 2025); the stub now anchors only π0.6/π0.6-MEM.
- Key content: **97.6% of pre-training is not mobile-manipulation data**; hierarchical inference (subtask → action chunk, one model); the **discrete-FAST-pretrain → flow-matching-post-train** recipe that KI and MolmoAct2 later adopt; at **104 training locations** the model matches a control trained on the test homes. Ablations: cross-embodiment data essential, web data buys OOD vocabulary. Beats π0 and π0-FAST+Flow. n=10/task in ablations — flagged per the audit.
- **The reconciliation the wiki needed**: π0.5's demonstrated generalization is environment/object-level (unseen homes, real); the 2026 collapses are instruction-level (paraphrase), on benchmark-finetuned variants. Both records are real. One line, now on both π0.5 pages: **the co-training recipe bought scene generalization but not instruction generalization** — anticipated by the paper's own "relatively simple prompts" limitation.
- **[Physical Intelligence](entities/physical-intelligence.md) refreshed** (updated 2026-05-25 → 2026-08-03): π-series table now has π0.5 as a primary-ingested row; new **"Founding team — the code-as-policy crossing"** section (Hausman/Ichter/Finn/Levine = the SayCan/Inner Monologue/Code as Policies cohort — the wiki's two rival architectures share their people); new "π0.5 as the standing 2026 baseline" section.

- The review's three standing watch items, all resolved to different degrees in one pass:
- **[SciFi-Benchmark](sources/scifi-benchmark-paper.md) — fully closed.** arXiv 2503.10706 (Sermanet/Majumdar/Sindhwani; 101 pp). Confirmed as the provenance of ASIMOV-Dilemmas-Scifi (its 9,056/53,384 corpus **is** that train split). Constitutions lift alignment 79.4%→95.8% and hold **92.3% under adversarial prompting** (vs 23.3% without) — the cluster's strongest evidence that prompt-level constitutions resist prompt-level attack. Sci-fi-derived constitutions top-perform on ASIMOV's real-world data. **All three works named on [DeepMind's safety page](sources/deepmind-gemini-robotics-safety-page.md) are now ingested**, and [Sindhwani](entities/vikas-sindhwani.md) is confirmed on all five papers of the safety program.
- **[Gemini Robotics On-Device 2 model card](sources/gemini-robotics-on-device-2-model-card.md) — partially closed.** The official card exists and was ingested: GR 1.5 tech + on-device Gemma lineage; **SO101 53.3% (v2) vs 6.7% (v1)**; Dexmate 75.6% vs 33.3%; stated limitation "controlling high-degree-of-freedom robots." **The envelope (params/memory/hardware/rate) remains unpublished** — a third-party ~0.25 s closed-loop figure was found but kept out of the [control-rate ladder](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md) as unofficial. The v1→v2 jump on SO101 (+46.6 pp) is the story: the on-device tier went from unusable to plausibly useful on the low-cost arm class in one generation.
- **LIBERO-PRO watch — upgraded, not closed.** Ai2's [vla-evaluation-harness](sources/vla-evaluation-harness-github.md) (ingested) supports LIBERO-Pro *and* MolmoAct2/GR00T N1.7/π0.5 in one dockerized system with a 2,456-model leaderboard — **the question is no longer blocked on tooling**. Its reproduction reports independently verify four LeRobot checkpoints at 96–100% of published standard-LIBERO scores (replication ≠ the memorization critique, noted). A search hit initially suggested LIBERO-PRO itself had expanded (GR00T-N1.6 rows, different numbers); **verified against arXiv v2 (2026-05-25): it has not** — the wiki's recorded numbers stand, and the divergent numbers trace to an un-ingested June 2026 adopter (arXiv 2606.27663), filed as a lead on [libero-pro-paper](sources/libero-pro-paper.md).
- Backlog: SciFi item closed; On-Device envelope item updated to partially-closed; LIBERO-PRO item upgraded to "unblocked on tooling."
