## [2026-08-03] ingest | MolmoAct v1 primary (arXiv 2508.07917) — the Ai2 line's last secondhand gap
- Prompted by a query ("what robots does Ai2 use for Molmo research?") that exposed the gap: MolmoAct v1 was known only via [VLA-0's table](sources/vla-0-paper.md), [MolmoAct2](sources/molmoact2-paper.md)'s description, and [Anthropic's eval](sources/anthropic-how-claude-performs-on-robotics-tasks.md). Now [primary-ingested](sources/molmoact-paper.md).
- **The query's answer, now first-hand: v1 is Franka-only** — single-arm (incl. a DROID-style mobile mount carried through living rooms/kitchens/bathrooms/bedrooms) + a bimanual Franka rig; LIBERO + SimplerEnv in sim. The YAM / SO-100 / DROID-fleet breadth arrived with MolmoAct2.
- **What the secondhand record had missed:** the ARM three-stage pipeline's stages are *independently decodable* (depth map, trajectory overlay, actions), and the distinctive contribution is **steering by editing the visual trace** — found more reliable than language corrections. Also: the **MolmoAct Dataset** (10,689 Franka trajectories, 93 tasks, five operators × two months) is the direct ancestor of MolmoAct2's released-dataset program, and the paper ran **arena-style Elo human evaluation of policies** — an early instance of preference-based policy evaluation predating [RoboArena](entities/roboarena.md)'s formalization.
- Numbers recorded with N: SimplerEnv zero-shot 70.5% (above π0, GR00T N1.5); LIBERO 86.6 (consistent with the 86.8 the wiki carried); real-world +10% single-arm / +22.7% bimanual task progression over π0-FAST at **25 trials/task** (fine-grained orderings don't separate at that n); +23.3% OOD.
- **New open question filed:** whether MolmoAct2 retained editable-trace steerability — the v1's most distinctive capability is not visible in the wiki's v2 record.
- Updated: [MolmoAct](entities/molmoact.md) (secondhand-grounding note replaced; first-hand section added), [Ai2](entities/ai2.md), [Franka Panda](entities/franka-panda.md), [Molmo](entities/molmo.md), [chain-of-thought](concepts/learning/chain-of-thought.md), [index](index.md). Closes the "MolmoAct primary un-ingested" item carried in the backlog wrap-up notes since 2026-07-18.

## [2026-08-04] ingest | TurboVLA (arXiv 2607.27205) — the first VLA in the wiki with no LLM in the control loop
- Requested ingest of an arXiv link; PDF pulled to `raw/2607.27205.pdf` (16 pp, [HUST](entities/hust.md) + [Huawei](entities/huawei.md), 2026-07-29). Filed as [TurboVLA paper](sources/turbovla-paper.md).
- **The architectural claim.** The paper names the dominant design **V→L→A** — vision projected into a language model's token space, actions decoded from the resulting hidden state — and proposes **V+L→A**: [DINOv3](entities/dinov3.md) + BERT encoded independently, fused by 6 layers of bidirectional cross-attention initialized from [Grounding DINO](entities/grounding-dino.md), decoded by an [ACT](entities/act.md)-style parallel-query head. `H^L` is never formed. Filed as a new concept, [LLM-free VLA](concepts/learning/llm-free-vla.md), deliberately positioned as **orthogonal to the four action-head families** rather than as a fifth one — the families describe how actions leave an LLM, this describes whether one is present.
- **Numbers, with N and compute recorded at ingest** (per the [audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md)'s standing requests): [LIBERO](entities/libero.md) **97.7** at n=2,000; [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md) **60.2** at n=5,000; real [AgileX Piper](entities/agilex-piper.md) 4 tasks at n=40. **0.2 B params / 0.9 GB VRAM / 31.2 ms on an RTX 4090**; trained on **four RTX 4090s** with **no embodied pretraining** — the smallest training footprint attached to any top-tier LIBERO number here.
- **The audit inverted the paper's own emphasis.** LIBERO 97.7 vs π0.5 96.9 is a **tie** (p=0.12), as is vs OpenVLA-OFT and VLA-Adapter; the abstract's "matching or outperforming" survives only in its first word. But **RoboTwin 60.2 vs π0.5 57.0 *survives* (p=0.0012)** — a smaller gap that separates because the sample is 2.5× larger and the base rate near 58% leaves headroom that a 97% ceiling does not. Filed as **audit section E**, with the generalizable lesson: *prefer benchmarks with headroom*; LIBERO's saturation is a measurement problem independent of, and compounding with, the [LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md) critique.
- **The ablation is the paper's best content and is well-powered (n=2,000).** Removing language costs 26.9 pp with **LIBERO-Goal collapsing 97.4 → 11.6** while Object holds at 99.4 — the wiki's clearest measurement of both what instruction conditioning buys *and* how much of LIBERO is solvable from visual priors alone. A learned task-ID embedding recovers to 95.4 but stays a **statistically real** 2.3 pp short (p=0.0001), while BERT and T5-small are indistinguishable (p=0.23). Net: **language semantics are load-bearing; language *models* may not be.**
- **The control-rate ladder gained its first controlled slice.** Seven rows now come from one group, one RTX 4090, batch 1, official checkpoints. Two findings recorded: parameter count is a poor latency predictor ([Evo-1](entities/evo-1.md) at 0.8 B is 4.4× slower than TurboVLA at 0.2 B, and slower than π0.5 at 3.4 B — what costs time is *what is in the pathway*), and π0.5 on a 4090 runs at 10.7 Hz, roughly GR00T-on-Thor — changing the architecture moved a policy three times further than changing the silicon.
- **The deployability landscape gained a fifth axis.** Its axis 3 (latency) conflates *rate* with *the compute class required to reach it* — MolmoAct2's 55.8 Hz is an H100 figure, TurboVLA's 32 Hz is a consumer-GPU figure in 0.9 GB. Also the first entry where the policy's memory footprint is not the binding platform constraint: 0.9 GB clears an 8 GB [Orin Nano](entities/jetson-orin-nano.md), where GR00T-3B's 16 GB floor eliminates the board.
- **Named the decisive open question in three places.** TurboVLA is untested against [LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md) and is arguably the wiki's **most exposed** model to it — no embodied pretraining, no web-scale language priors, trained on LIBERO alone. The counter-hypothesis (grounding pretraining may transfer under object swaps better than an LLM latent) is equally live. Ai2's [eval harness](sources/vla-evaluation-harness-github.md) makes the test ~18 min/H100, so this is cheap and unclaimed.
- Created: [TurboVLA paper](sources/turbovla-paper.md), [TurboVLA](entities/turbovla.md), [LLM-free VLA](concepts/learning/llm-free-vla.md), [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md), [AgileX Piper](entities/agilex-piper.md), [Grounding DINO](entities/grounding-dino.md), [HUST](entities/hust.md), [Huawei](entities/huawei.md), [Xiang Bai](entities/xiang-bai.md), [Dingkang Liang](entities/dingkang-liang.md), [Evo-1](entities/evo-1.md), [UniVLA](entities/univla.md).
- Updated: [VLA models](concepts/learning/vla-models.md), [LIBERO](entities/libero.md), [DINOv3](entities/dinov3.md), [ACT](entities/act.md), [success-rate audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md), [control-rate ladder](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md), [VLA deployability landscape](syntheses/platforms/vla-deployability-landscape.md), [index](index.md).
- **New backlog items:** (1) run LIBERO-PRO on TurboVLA; (2) measure an LLM-free VLA on a Jetson — the ladder's highest-value missing row; (3) primary ingest of RoboTwin 2.0 (its randomized-scene setting is an unrun generalization test); (4) UniVLA primary for the latent-action/cross-embodiment thread; (5) verify TurboVLA checkpoint release.

## [2026-08-04] query | "Does a language exist to specify robot actions that is a subset of natural language?"
- User asked whether a human-readable, embodiment-agnostic action language exists that an LLM could emit as tokens. Answered from the wiki + targeted external search; filed as [Action representation languages](syntheses/agents/action-representation-languages.md).
- **The answer, in one line: several partial fits exist, none has both properties, and the two properties are *diverging*.** The term for what was asked for is a **controlled natural language** (CNL — cf. Attempto, ASD Simplified Technical English in aviation maintenance); **no robot-action CNL has been standardized** — no ISO, no ROS spec, no cross-lab vocabulary.
- **The spectrum table is the durable artifact:** free-form task language → semantic subtask ([π0.5](entities/pi-zero-5.md)) → RT-H language motions → PDDL/LTL → behavior trees → [code+API](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md) → [MolmoAct](entities/molmoact.md) visual traces → [VLA-0](entities/vla-0.md) action-as-text → [FAST](entities/fast-action-tokenization.md) tokens → unified latent codebook tokens → raw joints. Scored on readability, embodiment-agnosticism, and **who does the grounding** — the last column is where the argument lives.
- **Three wiki measurements bear on it, and two cut against the premise.** (1) [CaP-X](sources/cap-x-paper.md): success falls **monotonically** as human macros are stripped — so a rich readable vocabulary is a human-authored controller in a vocabulary costume; the artifact of value is the grounding of each term, not the term list. (2) [MolmoAct](sources/molmoact-paper.md): **editing a visual trace beat language correction** ("language commands can suffer from ambiguity") — a head-to-head between two *readable* intermediates in which the non-linguistic one won. (3) [TurboVLA](sources/turbovla-paper.md): a learned task-ID embedding (the degenerate CNL) reaches 95.4 vs semantic English 97.7, a **statistically real 2.3 pp** at n=2,000 — the measured cost of closing the vocabulary.
- **The 2026 update that should move a prior:** cross-embodiment work converged on **unified latent tokens** (UniT's RQ-VAE codebook, universal action tokenization, [UniVLA](entities/univla.md)) that are unreadable *by construction*. The field's best answer to "maps to any embodiment" purchased portability **with** readability — which is precisely the trade the question hoped to avoid.
- **What actually deploys** is semantic subtask English ("pick up the cutting board") — π0.5 internally, [Helix](sources/helix-blog.md)/[GR00T](entities/nvidia-groot.md) across S2/S1, [TurboVLA](entities/turbovla.md)'s proposed hierarchy. Not a specified language; free-form English grounded by a learned per-embodiment policy. **The language is the easy part; the interpreter is the hard part.**
- Page ends with a two-layer design sketch (portable specified semantic layer + per-embodiment declarative grounding contract, the shape [Rosetta](sources/rosetta-github.md) already has for ROS 2 data) and a **one-training-run experiment**: add a CNL condition to TurboVLA's instruction-encoding ablation between free-form English (97.7) and task-ID (95.4); ~1 pp separates at n=2,000 on four consumer GPUs.
- Cross-linked from [control abstraction levels](concepts/robotics/control-abstraction-levels.md), [code as policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md), [LLM-free VLA](concepts/learning/llm-free-vla.md), [VLA models](concepts/learning/vla-models.md); indexed in Highlights + Syntheses/Agents.
- **Coverage gaps this query exposed, filed as backlog:** (1) **RT-H** (arXiv 2403.01823) un-ingested — the single most relevant prior work, cited here from abstract + project page only; (2) **PDDL / LTL** task specification — no page, despite being the 50-year-old classical answer to this exact question; (3) **behavior trees** — no page, despite being the dominant *deployed* action-composition formalism; (4) **UniT / universal action tokenization** — un-ingested, would anchor a `latent-action-tokens` concept that UniVLA also points at; (5) **controlled natural languages** generally — the transferable prior art is in aerospace documentation, not robotics.

## [2026-08-04] ingest | RT-H (arXiv 2403.01823) — closing the gap the action-language synthesis opened
- Requested follow-up to the [action representation languages](syntheses/agents/action-representation-languages.md) query, which cited RT-H from its abstract and flagged it as the top ingest candidate. PDF pulled to `raw/2403.01823.pdf` (23 pp, DeepMind + Stanford). Filed as [RT-H paper](sources/rt-h-paper.md).
- **The ingest corrected the synthesis.** The page had said RT-H's vocabulary is "derived from the dataset, not specified in advance." Wrong in an interesting way: language motions are extracted **mechanically from proprioception** by a *fixed hand-designed procedure* — map each of 9 action dims to a spatial word, threshold small dims, compose survivors in magnitude order → **2,500+ phrases at zero annotation cost**. Human labeling was tried and rejected (inconsistent, and annotators misjudged direction from camera angles). So RT-H **is** a genuine controlled natural language: *specified grammar, induced lexicon* — a much better fit to the original question than the abstract suggests.
- **And it pins the embodiment coupling exactly.** The 9 dims are *this* robot's (arm deltas + **mobile base** + parallel gripper). Change morphology → change the extraction → change the entire lexicon. **The grammar ports; the vocabulary cannot.** The sharpest illustration in the wiki of why readability and portability keep failing to co-occur: the readable names are names for *one robot's degrees of freedom*.
- **The decisive new evidence — and it cuts *for* the readable-language premise, unlike the synthesis's first two measurements.** RT-H-**OneHot** relabels the same 2,500 motions as integers, holding the partition fixed, and performance drops **substantially**: *"the structure of language greatly improves language motion and action prediction."* This is the **same experiment** as [TurboVLA](sources/turbovla-paper.md)'s task-ID ablation (95.4 vs 97.7, p=0.0001) — 2024 vs 2026, 55B [PaLI-X](entities/pali-x.md) vs 0.2 B [LLM-free](concepts/learning/llm-free-vla.md), real robot vs LIBERO — **and they agree**. Added to the synthesis as measurement 4; the page's top-line answer was rewritten around it. Untested confound recorded: RT-H's edge may come from PaLI-X's internet-scale prior, and the two designs have never been run head to head.
- **RT-H-Cluster is the near-miss worth keeping:** K-means over raw actions (a finer partition, integer labels) does slightly worse on average but **better on the hardest precision tasks** — finer clusters guide the decoder more but are harder to predict upstream. The abstraction tradeoff, measured inside one paper, and stated outright by the authors.
- Numbers recorded with N (8 tasks × 10 trials = **80**; the paper reports **95% Wilson CIs**, better hygiene than most sources here): +15 pp over [RT-2](entities/rt-2.md) **survives marginally** (p=0.043); corrections **40 → 63%** survives (p=0.0036); vs RT-2-IWR 13% survives (p<0.0001); RT-2-IWR's *degradation* 25→13 is **marginal** (p=0.053); object generalization 65 vs 55 at n=50 is a **TIE** (p=0.31) and should not be repeated as a win.
- **Most interesting unknown surfaced:** RT-H's own Future Work proposes using language motions to bridge **[OXE](entities/open-x-embodiment.md) embodiments and human video** — precisely the question the synthesis asks — and the 2026 field went to unified latent tokens instead. Whether the language route was tried and failed or never attempted is unrecorded. Named the cheapest test: re-run the extraction grammar on a different morphology and check lexicon compatibility.
- **Design insight added to the synthesis:** *specify the grammar, induce the lexicon.* The portable artifact is the extraction procedure, not the word list; each embodiment generates its own vocabulary by running it.
- Created: [RT-H paper](sources/rt-h-paper.md), [RT-H](entities/rt-h.md), [RT-2](entities/rt-2.md), [PaLI-X](entities/pali-x.md), [Dorsa Sadigh](entities/dorsa-sadigh.md), [Suneel Belkhale](entities/suneel-belkhale.md), [Ted Xiao](entities/ted-xiao.md).
- Updated: [action representation languages](syntheses/agents/action-representation-languages.md) (top-line answer, spectrum row, measurement 4, full RT-H section rewrite, design sketch, gaps), [index](index.md).
- **New gap exposed:** [RT-2](entities/rt-2.md) and **RT-1 have no pages** despite being the most-referenced un-ingested models in the wiki's VLA thread — RT-2 now has a stub built secondhand from RT-H, which is the same secondhand-grounding problem the MolmoAct ingest closed last week. PDDL / LTL and behavior trees remain uncovered.

## [2026-08-04] ingest | Six sources — the RT lineage, PDDL, behavior trees, and UniT
- A single batch closing **every backlog item** the previous two ingests named: RT-1, RT-2, PDDL, behavior trees, and UniT. All six feed [action representation languages](syntheses/agents/action-representation-languages.md), which is where the payoff landed.
- **[Behavior Trees in Robotics and AI](sources/behavior-trees-book.md)** (Colledanchise & Ögren, 198 pp) — read structurally. Six node types ticked from the root; formally **generalizes** FSMs, HFSMs, subsumption, teleo-reactive programs, decision trees. Modularity from **two-way control transfer** where an FSM transition is a one-way `goto`. Plus safety-by-construction, state-space proofs, **stochastic BTs → DTMCs** yielding success probability and expected completion time, PA-BT planning, GP/RL/LfD. Unusually candid disadvantages. Founds [behavior trees](concepts/robotics/behavior-trees.md).
- **[RT-1](sources/rt-1-paper.md)** — the lineage's ancestor. 35M at **3 Hz** (the rate was a *design constraint*: FiLM-EfficientNet-B3 + TokenLearner 81→8 tokens, identity-initialized FiLM); 130k demos / 700+ tasks / 13 robots / 17 months; **97 seen / 76 unseen** vs Gato 65/52, BC-Z 72/19 — with **baselines retrained on RT-1's data**, and **BC-Z XL worse than BC-Z**. Absorbs sim data free and **Kuka IIWA data for 22 → 39%** on new bin-picking: the wiki's earliest cross-embodiment datapoint, achieved by *just mixing data* — exactly what UniT later argues breaks at humanoid scale.
- **[RT-2](sources/rt-2-paper.md)** — **the paper that coined "VLA."** Co-fine-tuning is the load-bearing choice (precursor to [π0.5](entities/pi-zero-5.md) and [KI](concepts/learning/knowledge-insulation.md)). **Seen-task performance is flat vs RT-1; the entire gain is generalization** (unseen 62 vs 32). Emergent symbol/reasoning/human-recognition at 3×; first embodied CoT. And the disclosure that founded off-board serving: *"infeasible to run on standard desktop machines… a multi-TPU cloud service queried over the network"* — **55B at 1–3 Hz**. [RT-2 entity](entities/rt-2.md) promoted from the secondhand stub created hours earlier.
- **[RT-2 blog](sources/rt-2-deepmind-blog.md)** ingested as a communication artifact. **Contradiction recorded:** the blog headlines **3× generalization**; the paper reports **~2×**, reserving 3× for the *emergent* eval. Every other number checks out. The blog also omits the 1–3 Hz cloud-serving fact and the limitations entirely.
- **[Generalized Planning in PDDL](sources/generalized-planning-pddl-llm-paper.md)** (Silver, …, Tenenbaum, Kaelbling; AAAI 2024) — GPT-4 writes Python *generalized planners*. Debugging essential, CoT non-uniform, two training tasks often enough. **PG3 scores 1.00 on six domains and 0.00 on the one novel domain where GPT-4 gets 0.60** — the wiki's code-as-policy shape (graceful degradation vs cliff), now visible with **no perception involved at all**. Founds [symbolic task planning](concepts/agents/symbolic-task-planning.md).
- **[UniT](sources/unit-paper.md)** (XPENG + Tsinghua + HKU) — the counter-proposal to a readable action language, which calls its codebook *"a unified physical language."* Tri-branch cross-reconstruction into a shared **RQ-VAE codebook**, on the insight that different kinematics share *visual consequences*. **+18.9 pp from the objective alone** (66.7 vs 47.8, identical architecture, n=1,200); **~10× data efficiency**; **0 → 60% zero-shot** from human video with emergent waist rotation; **1.7× vs FAST's 10.7×** degradation under noise. Honest negative: the unified interface **only pays when embodiments are mixed** (≈ raw actions on single-embodiment DROID). Founds [latent action tokens](concepts/learning/latent-action-tokens.md).
- **The synthesis gained two structural findings.**
- **(1) The names result now has three independent confirmations, across paradigms sharing nothing:** RT-H-OneHot (2024, PaLI-X 55B, real robot), TurboVLA task-ID (2026, 0.2 B LLM-free, LIBERO), **PDDL No-Names (2023, GPT-4 program synthesis, no robot, no perception)** — 0.90 → **0.10**. PDDL is the sharpest case because the loss is *collapse*, and because PDDL is **formally complete without names**: a classical planner solves the renamed domain identically. The names exist purely for humans, and an LLM needs them for the same reason. Confound recorded: all three consume text-pretrained models, so this measures what such models need, not what is intrinsically necessary.
- **(2) The question is two questions.** Behavior trees make the **composition** readable while staying agnostic about the **action** — the only row on the spectrum where readability and portability co-occur, because a BT never tried to name the robot's DoFs. **The architecture that would answer the original question is a BT with latent-token policies at its leaves, and nobody has built it.** BTs are also a 20-year-old formally-analyzed candidate for the [execution rail that ships empty](syntheses/agents/guardrails-for-robot-agents.md); those two facts currently sit in different literatures.
- Created: 6 source pages; concepts [behavior trees](concepts/robotics/behavior-trees.md), [symbolic task planning](concepts/agents/symbolic-task-planning.md), [latent action tokens](concepts/learning/latent-action-tokens.md); entities [RT-1](entities/rt-1.md), [UniT](entities/unit.md), [XPENG Robotics](entities/xpeng-robotics.md), [Colledanchise](entities/michele-colledanchise.md), [Ögren](entities/petter-ogren.md), [Silver](entities/tom-silver.md), [Tenenbaum](entities/josh-tenenbaum.md), [Kaelbling](entities/leslie-kaelbling.md).
- Updated: [action representation languages](syntheses/agents/action-representation-languages.md) (top-line answer, spectrum, three-way names section, new composition-vs-action section, cross-embodiment section, gaps), [RT-2](entities/rt-2.md) (stub → primary), [VLA models](concepts/learning/vla-models.md), [control-rate ladder](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md), [index](index.md).
- **New gaps exposed:** (1) **TAMP** — the tradition that bridges symbolic specs to motion, directly relevant to PDDL's core limitation, uncovered; (2) **BehaviorTree.CPP / Nav2 BT navigator** — the actual ROS 2 implementations, uncovered; (3) **LTL / temporal-logic specification** still uncovered; (4) **BT-over-VLA** has no source and is the most valuable missing thing; (5) universal action tokenization and UniVLA remain secondhand.

## [2026-08-04] ingest | BehaviorTree.CPP + Nav2 BT navigator — the BT thread's implementations
- Top item from the same day's [backlog](backlog.md), and the prerequisite for the BT-over-VLA architecture the [action-representation synthesis](syntheses/agents/action-representation-languages.md) names as its most valuable gap. Both ingested; **`behaviortree.dev` 403s unauthenticated fetchers** — retrieved via curl with a browser user-agent, and Nav2's docs needed HTML stripping because the fetch summarizer only saw nav menus.
- **[BehaviorTree.CPP](sources/behaviortree-cpp-docs.md) v4.9** (MIT, C++17) **retires the [book](sources/behavior-trees-book.md)'s stated disadvantage** that *"BT tools are less mature."* Asynchronous actions are **first-class** — directly answering the book's warning that single-threaded engines can't give full BT semantics. Runtime XML trees, node plugins, logging/profiling/replay, Groot2 editor. v3→v4 changes captured (`SequenceStar`→**`SequenceWithMemory`**, `SubTreePlus`→default `SubTree`, scripting + pre/post conditions replacing `SetBlackboard`, `BTCPP_format="4"`).
- **The port/blackboard model is the load-bearing find.** *"Custom TreeNodes… are not conceptually different from **functions**"* — the book's two-way-control-transfer argument restated as an API. Type-safe input/output ports over a shared key/value blackboard; XML separates literal from reference (`"hello world"` vs `"{greetings}"`). **This is the interface a BT-over-VLA needs**: a VLA node takes its instruction on a typed input port (literal, or `{current_subtask}` from an upstream planner) and writes results back for guard conditions to read — a **typed function-call boundary around an opaque policy**, which is exactly what the [guardrails](syntheses/agents/guardrails-for-robot-agents.md) thread says an execution rail requires.
- **[Nav2](sources/nav2-behavior-trees-docs.md) is the production instance**, and the more valuable of the two. Full default-tree XML captured (`navigate_to_pose_w_replanning_and_recovery.xml`, BTCPP v4).
- **Nav2 had to invent control nodes** — the strongest available evidence on where the classical formalism falls short: **PipelineSequence** (re-ticks *all prior* children, *"resembling the flow of water in a pipe"* — so the planner keeps replanning while the controller follows), **RecoveryNode**, **RoundRobin** (retains position across ticks → escalating recovery tries something *new* each failure), **NonblockingSequence** (re-ticks succeeded children so they *"do not latch a stale state"*), plus `RateController` / `DistanceController` / `SpeedController`. **Almost every addition is about *when* things tick, not about control flow — the formalism's gap is temporal, not structural**, which is the operational form of the book's own "checking all conditions can be expensive" caveat.
- **Two-tier, cause-selected recovery** is the design worth stealing: each primary behavior has its own `RecoveryNode` whose remedy is gated by a `WouldA…RecoveryHelp` condition reading *that behavior's error code*, so the remedy is chosen by the failure's cause and only attempted if it could plausibly help. System-level escalation (clear costmaps → Spin → Wait → BackUp) runs only after contextual recovery fails, bounded at 6 retries. `ReactiveFallback` with `<GoalUpdated/>` first means **a new goal preempts recovery immediately**. Runtime plugin selection writes chosen planner/controller IDs to the blackboard — **the tree is the stable interface, implementations are hot-swappable**.
- **A standing wiki finding is now qualified.** The [guardrails synthesis](syntheses/agents/guardrails-for-robot-agents.md) concluded the **execution rail ships empty** in every stack examined. Nav2's default tree is a counterexample in plain sight — `ValidatePath`, `IsGoalNearby`, `WouldAControllerRecoveryHelp`, `GoalUpdated` are **world-state preconditions gating actions**, with bounded retries and declared escalation, in diffable XML. It stays *scoped to navigation*, is safety-**adjacent** rather than safety-**enforcing**, and Nav2's docs make **no safety argument at all** — so the finding stands, restated as *"the mechanism ships, applied to a different problem."*
- **The BT-over-VLA is now fully scaffolded and still unbuilt.** Engine, typed port interface, and a production reference tree all exist; **no Nav2 leaf contains a learned policy** — every one is a classical planner, controller, or scripted behavior. That substitution is the entire remaining experiment.
- New ladder entry: **Nav2 replans at 1 Hz** (`RateController hz="1.0"`) — a deliberate, shipped deliberative-tier rate in Band C.
- Created: [BehaviorTree.CPP docs](sources/behaviortree-cpp-docs.md), [Nav2 BT docs](sources/nav2-behavior-trees-docs.md), [BehaviorTree.CPP entity](entities/behaviortree-cpp.md).
- Updated: [behavior trees](concepts/robotics/behavior-trees.md) (new implementations section; tooling disadvantage struck), [Nav2](entities/nav2.md) (BT architecture section), [guardrails for robot agents](syntheses/agents/guardrails-for-robot-agents.md) (execution-rail qualification), [action representation languages](syntheses/agents/action-representation-languages.md), [control-rate ladder](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md), [backlog](backlog.md), [index](index.md).
- **Open:** the **BT tick rate** was not found (only `RateController hz="1.0"` for replanning) and is what determines the tree's reactivity — belongs on the ladder. `PersistentSequence` / `PauseResumeController` semantics uncaptured. **`BehaviorTree.ROS2`** (action/service node wrappers) is the natural next read. Groot2's license unconfirmed. No BT performance characterization (tick overhead, practical tree size). The book's **stochastic-BT reliability analysis has never been applied to Nav2's tree**, despite that tree being exactly its target object.

## [2026-08-07] ingest | HAI Issue Brief — The World Model and Spatial Intelligence Era
- Created [HAI Issue Brief — The World Model and Spatial Intelligence Era](sources/hai-world-model-spatial-intelligence-brief.md) (Stanford HAI Policy & Society, July 2026; nine authors incl. Fei-Fei Li)
- **The wiki's first prescriptive policy document.** Central thesis: foundation-model governance governs *outputs*, agentic governance governs *authority to act*, and world models add a third object — **the validity of the learned environment itself**.

Corroborated: the evaluation patchwork, the per-architecture failure split, the renderer/simulator/planner collapse into unified models. Complicated: "planners are least mature" is right largely *because the counter-evidence is unreliable*; the shared-action-data market-failure claim sits next to Open X-Embodiment / DROID / LeRobot Hub; the cost-reduction claim has no numbers on either side.

## [2026-08-08] ingest | WorldArena + WorldArena 2.0 + WorldRoamBench — the world-model measurement cluster
- Sourced in response to the [HAI brief](sources/hai-world-model-spatial-intelligence-brief.md)'s open questions; three papers pulled into `raw/` and ingested as one cluster.
- Created [WorldArena paper](sources/worldarena-paper.md), [WorldArena 2.0 paper](sources/worldarena-2-paper.md), [WorldRoamBench paper](sources/worldroambench-paper.md)
- New entities: [WorldArena](entities/worldarena.md), [WorldRoamBench](entities/worldroambench.md), [Ctrl-World](entities/ctrl-world.md)
- **The perception–functionality gap, quantified.** EWMScore correlates r = 0.825 with human judgment, 0.600 with data-engine utility, **0.360 with action planning**. WorldRoamBench finds the same thing in a different task domain: "high visual quality ≠ good action following," largely independent.
- **Closed an open question from yesterday.** The wiki recorded "teaching to a flawed test" as an un-measured risk; WorldArena measures it — learned policy evaluators report **consistently higher** success rates than the simulator ("partial overfitting to successful trajectories"). Ranking survives (Ctrl-World r = 0.986), levels don't. [Veo](entities/veo.md) reports the opposite sign, still unexplained. Corrected [world-model evaluation](concepts/world-models/world-model-evaluation.md) and [sim-to-real transfer](concepts/learning/sim-to-real-transfer.md).
- **Genie 3 has no paper — that is the state of the world, not an ingest gap.** No params, architecture, or training corpus published. But [WorldRoamBench](entities/worldroambench.md) gives the wiki its first measured numbers: 1st of 10 in FPV (73.81), **winning on memory while placing 7th on action following**. Rewrote [Genie 3](entities/genie-3.md).
- **Vendor claims vs independent measurement, again.** [Genie Envisioner](entities/genie-envisioner.md) entered this wiki on an AGIBOT announcement claiming minute-scale stable rollouts; independently benchmarked it is **last of 14** on EWMScore and 0%/0% on both visuotactile tasks. Flagged on the page with the counter-caveats.
- Filed synthesis: [What world models are measurably good for](syntheses/world-models/what-world-models-are-measurably-good-for.md) — four roles, four verdicts; **learned dynamics are good enough to shape a policy, not to be one**; the text-vs-action-conditioned Cosmos natural experiment; and the largest remaining hole — **no JEPA-family model is evaluated by any of these benchmarks**, because 16 of WorldArena's metrics score video.
- Updated [world-model evaluation](concepts/world-models/world-model-evaluation.md), [world-model simulators](concepts/world-models/world-model-simulators.md), [sim-to-real transfer](concepts/learning/sim-to-real-transfer.md), [robot policy evaluation](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md), [Genie 3](entities/genie-3.md), [Genie Envisioner](entities/genie-envisioner.md), [Veo](entities/veo.md), [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md), [π0.5](entities/pi-zero-5.md), [NVIDIA Cosmos](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md), [LIBERO](entities/libero.md), [world-model policy vs wiki evidence](syntheses/society/world-model-policy-vs-wiki-evidence.md)
- Backlog still open: WorldScore / WorldModelBench / VBench / VideoPhy / PhyGenBench primaries; the Ctrl-World paper (arXiv 2510.10125); a primary World Labs / Marble source.

## [2026-08-08] ingest | Action-relevant latents + Latent video prediction — closing the JEPA evaluation gap
- Sourced in response to the gap flagged in yesterday's [WorldArena cluster](syntheses/world-models/what-world-models-are-measurably-good-for.md); dedup check found [JEPA-WMs](sources/jepa-wms-paper.md) (arXiv 2512.24497) was already ingested, so only two of the candidates were new.
- Created [What Makes Video World Model Latents Action-Relevant](sources/action-relevant-latents-paper.md) (SNU, arXiv 2606.07687), [Latent Video Prediction Learns Better World Models](sources/latent-video-prediction-better-world-models-paper.md) (Melbourne + Monash, arXiv 2605.15618)
- **Correction.** The claim that the JEPA family is "entirely unmeasured" was wrong — [stable-worldmodel](sources/stable-worldmodel-paper.md) and [JEPA-WMs](sources/jepa-wms-paper.md) already measure it, on planning success rather than video quality. The accurate diagnosis is **incommensurability**: two literatures, two instrument sets, neither runnable on the other's models. Corrected in [what world models are measurably good for](syntheses/world-models/what-world-models-are-measurably-good-for.md) and [world-model policy vs wiki evidence](syntheses/society/world-model-policy-vs-wiki-evidence.md), with the full table now in [world-model evaluation](concepts/world-models/world-model-evaluation.md).
- **The first shared instruments.** Both new sources work by freezing the representation and probing it — the one level at which pixel and latent models compare directly. **Pixel fidelity and action recoverability are orthogonal**: at ~20 dB PSNR frozen action R² spans −0.01 to +0.46, and the highest-PSNR backbones (SDXL VAE, Cosmos-1 tokenizer) score lowest. This is [WorldArena](entities/worldarena.md)'s r = 0.360 restated one level down, now covering both families.
- **The JEPA advantage, decomposed.** V-JEPA 2 +ID 0.85 vs VideoMAE +ID 0.75 vs Web-DINO +ID 0.16 → temporal video pretraining is the big lever, the JEPA objective adds **~+0.10**. Real, and smaller than the JEPA literature implies. But V-JEPA is *not* interchangeable with image-SSL: Web-DINO and SigLIP 2 stay at 0.16–0.17 and go **negative on rotation** — a caution now recorded on [DINO-WM](entities/dino-wm.md).
- **Two findings worth reusing.** Action signal in V-JEPA 2 **peaks at layer 14 and decays to layer 22**, so VLAs taking final-layer features sample near the worst point. And "**stable ≠ usable**": VideoPrism holds cosine similarity >0.98 under patch dropout while collapsing to 2.7% top-1.
- Noted that the dissociation predates both 2026 benchmarks — **Tian, Finn & Wu (ICLR 2023)** showed perceptual metrics rank video predictors differently from control success.
- Updated [JEPA](concepts/world-models/jepa.md), [world-model evaluation](concepts/world-models/world-model-evaluation.md), [generative-video vs JEPA](syntheses/world-models/generative-video-vs-jepa-world-models.md), [V-JEPA 2](entities/v-jepa-2.md), [DINO-WM](entities/dino-wm.md), [NVIDIA Cosmos](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md), [LIBERO](entities/libero.md)
- Still open: nobody has run a JEPA-family model through WorldArena's **functional** roles (data engine / policy evaluator / RL environment / action planner). Also uningested: Nilaksh et al. "Reconstruction or semantics?" (2605.06388), RoboTrustBench (2606.01600), Tian/Finn/Wu (ICLR 2023).

## [2026-08-08] ingest | VP² (ICLR 2023) + Reconstruction or Semantics? — the origin and the counter-evidence
- Created [VP² — A Control-Centric Benchmark for Video Prediction](sources/vp2-paper.md) (Tian, Finn & Wu, ICLR 2023), [Reconstruction or Semantics?](sources/latent-space-robotic-world-models-paper.md) (Nilaksh, Jha, Zholus & Chandar — Mila, arXiv 2605.06388)
- **VP² is the origin of the wiki's most-repeated 2026 finding, three years early.** Perceptual metrics mis-rank video predictors for control — and worse than "weak correlation," the **sign is task-dependent**: best FVD in the study (4.9) → 10% control success; worst (51.7) → 80%. SSIM spans 0.2 points while success spans 32%→82%. Everything in the [WorldArena cluster](entities/worldarena.md) is a rediscovery at scale, and neither 2026 benchmark cites it as an ancestor.
- VP² also has two design ideas the 2026 benchmarks lack: a **one-function model interface** and a **simulator-as-model upper bound** separating model failure from planner failure. Its scaling results are still uncontested — **6M→300M params buys no control gain**, data plateaus early, and diffusion cost 220 s/forward pass vs FitVid's 5.63 s.
- **Partly closed a standing open question.** The wiki asked what happens when a JEPA representation runs through WorldArena's functional roles. Nilaksh et al. run exactly the **policy-evaluator** role — OpenVLA-7B rolled out inside six world models differing only in frozen encoder — and semantic latents roughly **double** VLA success over VAE latents (0.362 vs 0.169). [V-JEPA 2.1](entities/v-jepa-2.md) strongest overall. Scoping: these are diffusion WMs *using* V-JEPA's latent space, not JEPA world models.
- Sharpest single finding: at larger DiT scale, VAE and Cosmos close the *policy-success* gap but still lag on CEM action recovery, IDM r, and classifier accuracy — **scale buys the parts of the problem that go through pixels and not the parts that go through dynamics.**
- **Contradiction flagged.** The two probe studies disagree on whether frozen image-SSL features (Web-DINO, SigLIP 2) are an adequate control substrate: 0.16–0.17 action R² and negative rotation in [action-relevant latents](sources/action-relevant-latents-paper.md), vs IDM Pearson r = 0.820 (~V-JEPA's 0.829) here. Candidate reconciliations (Pearson r vs R², spatial patch vs mean-pooled features, real vs sim data, aggregate vs per-DoF) recorded on [JEPA](concepts/world-models/jepa.md) and [DINO-WM](entities/dino-wm.md) — unresolved, and it bears directly on DINO-WM's design choice.
- WorldArena is now being **reused as infrastructure**: Nilaksh et al. adopt its perceptual/geometric scores as a metric component three months after publication.
- Updated [world-model evaluation](concepts/world-models/world-model-evaluation.md), [JEPA](concepts/world-models/jepa.md), [what world models are measurably good for](syntheses/world-models/what-world-models-are-measurably-good-for.md), [V-JEPA 2](entities/v-jepa-2.md), [DINO-WM](entities/dino-wm.md), [NVIDIA Cosmos](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md), [Chelsea Finn](entities/chelsea-finn.md), [WorldArena](entities/worldarena.md), [robosuite](entities/robosuite.md)
- Open: nobody has run VP² on a modern world model despite its one-function interface; and JEPA representations remain untested as data engine / RL environment / action planner.

## [2026-08-13] ingest | Sourccey (Vulcan Robotics) + X-VLA — a research VLA reaches a consumer product
- Created [X-VLA paper](sources/xvla-paper.md) (Zheng, Li et al., AIR Tsinghua + Shanghai AI Lab, arXiv 2510.10274), [Vulcan Robotics — Sourccey product site](sources/vulcan-robotics-sourccey-site.md), [sourccey-hardware GitHub repository](sources/sourccey-hardware-repo.md)
- New entities: [X-VLA](entities/x-vla.md), [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md), [Vulcan Robotics](entities/vulcan-robotics.md), [Nick Maselli](entities/nick-maselli.md), [Florence-2](entities/florence-2.md)
- New concept: [Soft-prompt cross-embodiment conditioning](concepts/learning/soft-prompt-cross-embodiment.md)
- **The two sources turned out to be one story.** Sourccey's spec page lists "Starting AI: XVLA with 4 micromodels" — the user submitted both URLs, and the arXiv paper is the model the robot ships. X-VLA's flagship real-world result *is* cloth folding (33 folds/hr from 1,200 demos on bimanual AgileX); Sourccey's flagship task is laundry folding. Vulcan picked the model whose headline demo is its own.
- **Cross-embodiment heterogeneity is a conditioning problem, not an architecture problem.** X-VLA runs a 2024-vintage [Florence-2](entities/florence-2.md) encoder and 24 *plain* self-attention blocks — no DiT, no cross-attention, no MoE — and beats 7–9 B models on five of six benchmarks at 0.9 B. Its ablation says where the points came from: swapping DiT for a plain encoder **cost** 2.1 pts; disentangled encoding (+16.7), [soft prompts](concepts/learning/soft-prompt-cross-embodiment.md) (+9.2), scaling (+15.8), two-step adaptation (+6.2) supplied the rest.
- **The sharpest negative result in the wiki's scaling coverage.** Naively adding 290 K episodes of mixed-embodiment data **dropped** Simpler-WidowX from 39.6 to 25.0, and only reached 89.6 once alignment, intention abstraction, balanced sampling, and prompts were in place. **Cross-embodiment data scaling is conditional on the conditioning mechanism** — a scaling curve measured without it slopes the wrong way. Recorded in [scaling laws — VLAs](concepts/learning/scaling-laws-vla.md), which previously said the field had exactly one scaling law; X-VLA adds a second (three axes, no fitted form) plus a **validation-error proxy at R² = −0.925** that lets scaling studies skip rollouts.
- **Closed a standing RoboTwin open question.** The wiki recorded that nobody had reported the domain-randomized setting. X-VLA reports both: **domain randomization costs every model 20–31 points** (X-VLA 70.0→39.0, π0 46.4→16.4, RDT 34.5→13.7), the ranking survives, and per-task numbers show total collapses hiding inside the average (`Place Object Basket` 50.0 → **0.0**). X-VLA's π0 and RDT clean figures match [TurboVLA](sources/turbovla-paper.md)'s independently — mild evidence both papers run the benchmark the same way.
- **DROID supplies an accidental controlled experiment.** X-VLA registers DROID twice as separate data sources differing *only* in which camera is designated main. The two learned prompts **intermingle** in t-SNE rather than separating — evidence that soft prompts encode configuration similarity, not dataset identity. Recorded on [DROID](entities/droid.md) and [Franka Panda](entities/franka-panda.md) (39.1% of the pretraining mixture is one 7-DOF arm, worth remembering when "cross-embodiment" generalization is assessed).
- **The most interesting engineering question is unpublished and about to be field-tested.** X-VLA aligns every embodiment to absolute SE(3) EEF pose, and **all seven pretraining embodiments have ≥6 DOF**. Sourccey's arms are **5 DOF + gripper** — no wrist yaw, so their reachable poses are a lower-dimensional manifold in that action space. Soft prompts are exactly the mechanism meant to absorb this and nothing published tests it. Flagged on [X-VLA](entities/x-vla.md), [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md), the concept page, and the synthesis.
- **Two things Vulcan's marketing doesn't reconcile, both flagged.** (1) The mecanum base is **open-loop PWM with no encoders** — no odometry — while the Oct 2026 roadmap item is "improved SLAM tools"; mecanum is the drive type most prone to lateral slip, i.e. where scan matching most needs a dead-reckoning prior. (2) A **0.9 B policy is advertised beside a Raspberry Pi 5** that cannot run it; the reconciliation is buried under "External AI — rented compute is planned," making Sourccey the same **PC-does-inference, Pi-relays** platform as [XLeRobot](entities/xlerobot.md), with a subscription.
- **Contradiction flagged.** The [Vulcan site](sources/vulcan-robotics-sourccey-site.md) and press coverage both say "public STLs." The [repository](sources/sourccey-hardware-repo.md) ships **115 STEP files and no STLs**, and its own README stresses STEP is not print-ready. Also absent: BOM, wiring, assembly sequence, and **URDF** (which must exist internally — the advertised Oculus IK needs one). The advertised "Electrical" repo does not exist in the org.
- **A licensing divergence worth watching.** Sourccey's hardware is **CERN-OHL-S-2.0** (strongly reciprocal) where [SO-ARM101](entities/so-arm101.md) / [LeKiwi](entities/lekiwi.md) / [XLeRobot](entities/xlerobot.md) are all Apache 2.0 — while Vulcan's *software* is Apache 2.0. First copyleft hardware in the wiki's low-cost manipulator coverage.
- Filed synthesis: [Sourccey vs XLeRobot](syntheses/platforms/sourccey-vs-xlerobot.md) — the two converge on an identical component list from opposite directions. Sourccey wins on shipped policy, vertical lift, standard LiDAR, integration; XLeRobot wins on **2.4× battery energy** (288 vs ~120 Wh), reproducibility, licensing, compute headroom, and having a price. Includes a wiki-derived payload estimate (**~0.3–0.5 kg sustained**, from shoulder-pitch torque over the 0.5 m distal chain).
- Also documented: **LeRobot's `lerobot_robot_*` third-party plugin convention** (auto-import by package prefix — a better path than fork-and-patch, recorded on [LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md)), and the current 21-policy zoo including upstream `xvla`.
- Updated [VLA models](concepts/learning/vla-models.md), [flow matching](concepts/learning/flow-matching.md), [scaling laws — VLAs](concepts/learning/scaling-laws-vla.md), [LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md), [FeeTech](entities/feetech.md), [Raspberry Pi 5](entities/raspberry-pi-5.md), [XLeRobot](entities/xlerobot.md), [LIBERO](entities/libero.md), [SimplerEnv](entities/simplerenv.md), [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md), [AgiBot](entities/agibot.md), [DROID](entities/droid.md), [AgileX Piper](entities/agilex-piper.md), [Franka Panda](entities/franka-panda.md), [π0](entities/pi-zero.md), [open-source robot AI projects](syntheses/platforms/open-source-robot-ai-projects.md)
- Open / backlog: **Sourccey's price, runtime, and open-source completeness all resolve Sep–Nov 2026 — re-check.** Which X-VLA checkpoint ships, and are the weights open? Soft-Fold dataset release. Prompt *retrieval* for zero-shot embodiment transfer (proposed, never run). No independent replication of soft prompts yet. **DIMOS** (`dimensionalOS/dimos`, forked as `dimos-vulcan`) — an agentic robot OS, uncovered here, candidate for its own page. RoboMind and the primary RoboTwin paper remain un-ingested.

## [2026-08-13] ingest | DimOS (Dimensional Inc.) — the converged agentic pattern at industrial scale
- Sourced from the backlog item opened by this morning's Sourccey ingest (`dimos-vulcan` was an unexplained fork in Vulcan's org). Created [DimOS GitHub repository](sources/dimos-github.md)
- New entities: [DimOS](entities/dimos.md), [Dimensional Inc.](entities/dimensional-inc.md)
- **The largest agentic-robotics codebase in the wiki, by an order of magnitude.** 3,874★ / 788 forks / 12+ contributors / Apache 2.0 / created Oct 2024 / committed daily. For comparison: [Rosetta](entities/rosetta.md) is 76★ and solo, and [Waddle](entities/waddle-labs.md) was one blog post. This is the first time the "LLM agent above a skill library" thesis can be read as code rather than as a claim.
- **Revised [LLM-agent architecture across stacks](syntheses/agents/llm-agent-architecture-across-stacks.md) — two of four convergences do not survive.** Convergence 1 said small open-weights Qwen is the effective default planner; DimOS defaults to **cloud `gpt-5.6-luna`** and treats Ollama as an alternate blueprint, because it targets an RTX 4070 / AGX Orin rather than an Orin Nano. Convergence 2 said every stack hand-rolls a JSON tool schema to avoid provider lock-in; DimOS standardizes on **MCP + LangGraph** — the same goal reached by *adopting the open protocol* rather than avoiding all protocols. Both original claims were inferences from three stacks that shared a constraint DimOS doesn't have; rescoped to the on-device tier rather than deleted. Convergences 3 and 4 hold across all four.
- **Narrowed that synthesis's headline finding rather than overturning it.** It claimed a clean bifurcation: no ingested stack combined LLM-agent control with imitation learning, LeRobot, or VLAs. DimOS breaks **three of the four absences** — Quest-teleop demonstration collection, `dimos dataprep` export to **[LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md) v3.0** (parquet + task-indexed `tasks.parquet`), and MuJoCo in the same CLI as hardware — but **no VLA runs in its control loop** (no π0, GR00T, OpenVLA, ACT, SmolVLA anywhere in the tree). Correct 2026-08 statement: **the two paths now share a data format, not a control path.**
- **Skill discovery by RPC introspection is a genuine advance on the pattern.** The other three stacks hand-maintain a prompt manifest plus a dispatcher (`PickupExecutor` FSM, `eval(f'self.{a}')`, ROS 2 services). DimOS decorates module methods with `@skill` and the agent enumerates them over RPC at startup, docstring becoming the tool description — the manifest is a projection of deployed code and cannot drift from it. Recorded on [LLM-agent architecture](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md) as the axis that has actually moved: hand-written manifest → agent-authored library → introspected from running code.
- **It also retires the `eval`-injection hazard** the synthesis flagged on the Hiwonder kits — skills are typed RPC calls with JSON-primitive parameters, nothing gets `eval`'d — while introducing a different one the docs don't discuss: `McpServer` exposes every robot skill to any client that can reach the endpoint. Security question moves from code injection to **endpoint authentication**.
- **"No ROS required" is ROS-*optional*, not anti-ROS.** ROS 2 is one of five interchangeable transports (LCM default, Zenoh, shared memory, DDS, ROS 2) under an unchanged module API, message names are deliberately ROS-shaped, and navigation is advertised "via both DimOS native and ROS." What it actually attacks is packaging — `pip install dimos` vs colcon workspaces. Recorded on [ROS 2](entities/ros2.md) as the wiki's first credible challenger entry.
- **`LeRobotDataset` is winning the format argument even where LeRobot loses the middleware argument.** Two unrelated projects now export into it from outside the ecosystem — [Rosetta](entities/rosetta.md) from ROS 2 via a YAML contract, DimOS from its own session DBs — neither depending on LeRobot for control. Noted on [LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md) and as a comparison table on [Rosetta](entities/rosetta.md).
- **Credit where due: the maturity labels are honest.** A four-level scale with **exactly one platform at 🟩 stable** (Go2), the humanoid everyone would demo at 🟨 beta, drones 🟧 alpha — plus a "Pre-Release Beta" banner and a `0.0.14b1` version string. Rare enough to make the rest of the claims easier to take at face value.
- **The standing evidentiary problem is unchanged and now starker.** Four stacks, zero published success rates. DimOS has ten built-in skills, five hardware classes, 277 MB of code, and **no benchmark, latency, or success-rate number anywhere**. Added as an open question to the synthesis: the pattern's claimed shippability advantage over VLAs rests on the absence of measurement on both sides.
- Also recorded: [Florence-2](entities/florence-2.md) appears a **second time** in a different role — encoder inside a policy (X-VLA) vs agent-callable perception service (DimOS); [Unitree Go2](entities/unitree-go2.md) is DimOS's only stable platform and its best-supported robot in this wiki; [Unitree G1](entities/unitree-g1.md) gets a full agentic-sim path needing no hardware; [xArm 7](entities/xarm-7.md) is where DimOS's agent stack and the wiki's imitation-learning coverage touch.
- **Closed yesterday's open question** on `dimos-vulcan`: it is a fork of this repo. Still unclear whether [Vulcan](entities/vulcan-robotics.md) intends it as a [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md) layer (1 star, last pushed 2026-07-03), but the fit is legible — DimOS's shipped 2D-LiDAR SLAM, spatial memory, and Quest→LeRobot pipeline map onto Sourccey's Oct–Nov 2026 roadmap, and Sourccey has no navigation layer of its own.
- Updated [LLM-agent architecture](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md), [agent skills](concepts/agents/agent-skills.md), [ROS 2](entities/ros2.md), [LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md), [Rosetta](entities/rosetta.md), [Waddle Labs](entities/waddle-labs.md), [Unitree Go2](entities/unitree-go2.md), [Unitree G1](entities/unitree-g1.md), [xArm 7](entities/xarm-7.md), [Florence-2](entities/florence-2.md), [Vulcan Robotics](entities/vulcan-robotics.md)
- Open / backlog: **nobody measures anything in this category** — a rollout-backed comparison of any two of these four stacks would be the highest-value experiment available. Does `@skill` discovery scale past ~10 skills before tool selection becomes the bottleneck? What action-space convention does `dimos dataprep` write, and is it compatible with SO-101/DROID-trained policies? Why is Jetson Orin Nano experimental (VRAM, ARM wheels, or thermals)? **`openFT-sensor`** (open force-torque, same org) un-ingested and relevant given how often low-cost platforms record position only. What is `gpt-5.6-luna`?

## [2026-08-13] ingest | RoboTwin 2.0 + RoboMIND + openFT — closing the exposed gaps
- Third session today; all three targets came off the backlog entry filed this morning. Created [RoboTwin 2.0 paper](sources/robotwin2-paper.md) (Chen et al., arXiv 2506.18088), [RoboMIND paper](sources/robomind-paper.md) (Wu, Hou, Liu et al., arXiv 2412.13877), [openFT-sensor GitHub repository](sources/openft-sensor-github.md)
- New entities: [RoboMIND](entities/robomind.md), [Tien Kung](entities/tien-kung.md), [OpenFT sensor](entities/openft-sensor.md)
- **The three sources converged on one finding nobody in them states, and it is the day's best result: the field's "embodiment-agnostic" action space is a parallel-gripper-shaped hole.**
  - **Top end excluded.** [RoboMIND](entities/robomind.md) holds **15,187 [Tien Kung](entities/tien-kung.md) dual-dexterous-hand trajectories**; [X-VLA](entities/x-vla.md) consumes RoboMIND for 19.9% of pretraining and **drops that split entirely** — structurally it must, since `xyz + Rot6D + binary gripper` cannot represent a multi-fingered hand. RoboMIND's own paper hits the same wall: OpenVLA was evaluated only on the Franka *"since the output of OpenVLA is the condition of one end effector."*
  - **Bottom end under-served.** [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md)'s embodiment-aware grasp adaptation moved the **6-DoF [Piper](entities/agilex-piper.md) from 2.4% to 25.1%** data-generation success while **7-DoF [Franka](entities/franka-panda.md) moved −0.1 and UR5 −0.5**. At 2.4%, a state-of-the-art generator **could not produce usable data for that arm at all**.
  - Together with this morning's [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md) 5-DoF question: **"cross-embodiment" in 2026 means cross-6-to-7-DoF-arm-with-a-parallel-gripper.** Both tails are where the interesting hardware lives. Recorded on [X-VLA](entities/x-vla.md), [RoboMIND](entities/robomind.md), [AgileX Piper](entities/agilex-piper.md), [Franka](entities/franka-panda.md), [Tien Kung](entities/tien-kung.md).
- **Franka's dominance is a selection effect worth naming.** 39.1% of X-VLA's pretraining, the only embodiment OpenVLA could be tested on in RoboMIND, and the platform that gained *nothing* from RoboTwin's grasp adaptation because it never had the problem. **The field's tooling is tuned on the platform that needs the least help**, and the gaps only appear on cheaper arms.
- **What VLA pretraining buys is robustness, not peak performance — now quantified.** Under RoboTwin's domain randomization, non-pretrained policies don't degrade, they die: [ACT](entities/act.md) 29.7 → **1.7**, [Diffusion Policy](entities/diffusion-policy.md) 28.0 → **0.6**, while pretrained RDT holds 13.7 and π0 16.3. And **DP3 beats every VLA on Easy (55.2) then collapses to 5.0** — the paper concedes its win *"partly stems from perfect point clouds and clean background segmentation in simulation."*
- **Fidelity did not close the sim-to-real gap; diversity did.** Finetuning on *clean* RoboTwin 2.0 data gave **no benefit** over released weights (RDT 18.8 → 14.6, worse). Randomized pretraining gave +31.9%/+29.3% relative, persisting even when the downstream task was then trained on clean data only. The authors' inference is right: since clean sim data doesn't help, this is **a robustness gap, not a real-to-sim gap**. Recorded on [sim-to-real transfer](concepts/learning/sim-to-real-transfer.md).
- **Two protocols now confirmed, and they are not equivalent instruments.** RoboTwin: **100 rollouts × 50 tasks = 5,000 per model per condition** at a ~40% base rate → ~2 pp separates. LIBERO: 2,000 at ~97% → >1.0 pp, and ten models sit inside 1.2 pp. **RoboTwin is the better instrument**; the audit assumed its protocol and could not verify it until now. Cross-validated: π0's 46.4 Easy is identical across the RoboTwin paper, [TurboVLA](sources/turbovla-paper.md), and [X-VLA](sources/xvla-paper.md).
- **A floor case for the sample-size rule: RoboMIND reports every result at n = 10.** ±30-point CIs per cell, so none of its ACT-vs-DP-vs-BAKU or per-embodiment orderings is a measurement. Not a criticism of a dataset paper — existence proofs are what it needs — but its tables are exactly the kind that get quoted as comparisons. Added to [robot policy evaluation](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md) and the [success-rate audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md).
- **Two opposite cures for heterogeneity, neither citing the other.** RoboMIND (Dec 2024): standardize collection so the data *is* homogeneous. X-VLA (Oct 2025): accept heterogeneity, give each source a learned prompt. X-VLA then consumes RoboMIND and **still assigns it four separate soft prompts** — camera rig and control frequency differ per setup even under one protocol. A mild negative result for the standardization thesis, visible only by reading both.
- **Corrected a venue claim.** [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md) was recorded as "ICML 2026"; the arXiv v2 carries no venue line. Marked unverified.
- **Flagged a headline-vs-table gap.** RoboTwin's abstract leads with "367% relative improvement"; that is the **unseen-background-cluttered row only**. The average across all four real-world configurations is **+24.4 points**. Both honest, one representative — the same class of vendor-style framing the wiki caught in the [RT-2 blog](sources/rt-2-deepmind-blog.md), this time inside a paper.
- **openFT is aimed at the wiki's most persistent hardware gap.** Every affordable platform here records position only — [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md) explicitly, [XLeRobot](entities/xlerobot.md) and [SO-ARM101](entities/so-arm101.md) by omission — while every contact-rich result comes from hardware costing 10× more. A Hall-effect 6-axis F/T sensor with open Gerbers and a JLCPCB-ready BOM is the cheapest published route out. **But**: 2 stars, created and last pushed the same day, **no LICENSE file** despite the README saying "open-source," and **no specs at all** — no range, noise floor, or bandwidth. The unaddressed risk specific to the principle: Hall sensors respond to stray magnetic fields, and a robot wrist is full of servo motors.
- Updated [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md) (secondhand → primary, substantially rewritten), [AgileX Piper](entities/agilex-piper.md), [Franka Panda](entities/franka-panda.md), [X-VLA](entities/x-vla.md), [DROID](entities/droid.md), [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md), [DimOS](entities/dimos.md), [Dimensional Inc.](entities/dimensional-inc.md), [sim-to-real transfer](concepts/learning/sim-to-real-transfer.md), [robot policy evaluation](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md), [success-rate audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md)
- Open: **nobody has run RoboTwin's generator against a 5-DoF arm** — the tier SO-ARM101 / XLeRobot / Sourccey occupy, and the gain grows as DoF falls. **No ablation isolates which of the five randomization axes carries the gain** (the texture library alone cost 20,000 SD generations). **Nobody has trained on RoboMIND's 5k failure trajectories or used its Isaac Sim digital twin.** What action space would make dexterous-hand data usable? And openFT: does the 4-cluster arrangement reject servo magnetic field?

## [2026-08-13] plan | 5-DoF arms in RoboTwin 2.0 — experiment scoped
- Filed [Can RoboTwin 2.0 generate data for a 5-DoF arm?](syntheses/projects/five-dof-arms-in-robotwin.md) against the top backlog item from this afternoon's ingest.
- **Feasibility verified, not assumed.** RoboTwin is **MIT, 2,721★, pushed 2026-08-10**; assets download from HF; there is a documented **7-step new-embodiment procedure** (CuRobo config → RoboTwin config → path → task config → `delta_matrix` → `global_trans_matrix` → dual-arm URDF); and the **SO-101 URDF exists** at `TheRobotStudio/SO-ARM100/Simulation/SO101/so101_new_calib.urdf` (Apache 2.0), with MuJoCo XML alongside.
- **The plan's main contribution is a falsifiable prediction, derived before running anything.** SO-101's joints are `shoulder_pan` (vertical yaw) + three **parallel pitch** joints + `wrist_roll` (about the tool axis). So the tool's world yaw is **slaved to position** via the pan angle — but `wrist_roll` *becomes* world yaw when the tool points down. Therefore **a 5-DoF SO-101-class arm is fully dexterous top-down and constrained laterally (radial approach only)** — the **opposite** of the Piper preference RoboTwin's augmentation was built around (*"a low-DoF platform like the Piper often relies on lateral grasps"*). Prediction: failures concentrate in non-radial lateral and articulated tasks (`handover_*`, `open_laptop`, `open_microwave`, `turn_switch`, `hanging_mug`) while top-down placement holds up. If failures are uniform instead, the kinematic story is wrong and reach is the real constraint.
- **The design element that makes it an experiment:** a **virtual 6-DoF twin** — same URDF with one `wrist_yaw` joint inserted, same links, masses, limits, and reach. Difference between the two is attributable to **DoF alone**, which no published result isolates. Plus a second control for reach (report `unreachable` as its own failure category; use RoboTwin's tabletop-height randomization).
- **Hard blocker found and recorded: this cannot run on the local WSL2 machine.** RoboTwin's own support matrix gives WSL **CPU sim ✅, GPU sim ❌, rendering ❌** — and rendering is not optional, since the dataset *is* camera observations and the VLM observer watches rendered rollouts. Needs native Linux + NVIDIA, or a rental. Unusual inversion also noted: RoboTwin documents collection stalls on **A/H-series** GPUs, so **prefer RTX-class over datacenter rentals** for this workload.
- All four outcome branches are worth reporting, including the deflationary one (5-DoF ≈ virtual-6-DoF → reach was the constraint, not DoF, which would partly undercut this wiki's own "gripper-shaped hole" thread). That is why the control is in the design.
- **Correction to this afternoon's ingest.** I flagged RoboTwin 2.0's "ICML 2026" venue as unverified because the arXiv PDF carries no venue line. The [official repo README](https://github.com/RoboTwin-Platform/RoboTwin) states **ICML 2026**; the page's original attribution was right. Fixed on [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md) and the [source page](sources/robotwin2-paper.md).

## [2026-08-13] scoping | 5-DoF RoboTwin experiment deferred — not on the path to a working XLeRobot
- Scored this morning's [experiment plan](syntheses/projects/five-dof-arms-in-robotwin.md) against the concrete goal of getting an XLeRobot navigating, picking and placing, and teleoperable. **Verdict: defer.** Recorded on the plan page (§3a) and the [backlog](backlog.md) rather than deleted.
- **Zero overlap with two of the three legs.** RoboTwin is a fixed-base tabletop manipulation *data generator* — it does nothing for navigation, and teleoperation via [LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md) leader-follower or Quest IK is a days-long solved job.
- **The decisive fact was already in the wiki and I under-weighted it while building the 5-DoF thread.** The gap is in the **cross-embodiment / synthetic-data** line, **not** the LeRobot single-platform line: [SmolVLA](entities/smolvla.md) is trained and validated on **SO-100/SO-101 — the same 5-DoF arm XLeRobot uses** — at 78.3% real-world multi-task, with **+26.6 pts** from community pretraining on an *out-of-distribution* SO-101 pick-and-place task. A working 5-DoF pick-and-place path exists and never touches RoboTwin, because SmolVLA sidesteps the problem by not being cross-embodiment at all. **The research finding is real; its practical consequence for a single-platform builder is much smaller than the thread implied.**
- **A [DGX Spark](entities/dgx-spark.md) does not unblock RoboTwin — it makes it more expensive.** GB10 is **ARM64** and RoboTwin's simulator is **SAPIEN**, whose wheels are x86_64-first; Spark runs the **CUDA 13** stack against RoboTwin's recommended **12.1**; and GB10 is datacenter-class silicon where RoboTwin documents collection stalls (issue #83 / SAPIEN #219). Verdict recorded: prefer **RTX-class**, and if a Spark is the only hardware, spend a spike day proving SAPIEN + CuRobo run on ARM64 before committing. The Spark's real strength in this stack is [policy training and serving](syntheses/projects/gr00t-spark-zmq-xlerobot.md).
- **The week splits, so this is not now-or-never**: embodiment bring-up ≈ 3–4 days (independently useful for synthetic XLeRobot data), control arm + analysis ≈ 3 days (pure research contribution). Doing the bring-up for practical reasons later makes the experiment a cheap add-on — one URDF variant and one extra generation run.
- Trigger to revive it: **when demonstration collection is demonstrably the bottleneck.** That wall is real when it arrives — X-VLA's cloth-folding dataset cost ~50–60 operator-hours for 1,200 episodes — just not the first one.

## [2026-08-13] plan | XLeRobot bring-up — nav + pick-and-place + teleop, sequenced
- Filed [XLeRobot — navigate, pick-and-place, teleoperate](syntheses/projects/xlerobot-nav-manip-teleop-bringup.md) after the [RoboTwin experiment](syntheses/projects/five-dof-arms-in-robotwin.md) was deferred as off-path. Written against a robot that is **assembled and already teleoperating**, so it starts past bring-up.
- **It is not a new framework — it executes [fleet-ladder](syntheses/projects/fleet-agentic-framework.md) steps 0 and 1** for the single XLeRobot. Step 0: record → train on the Spark → async deploy. Step 1: ROS 2 + a [Rosetta](entities/rosetta.md) contract, Nav2 and the learned pick as skills.
- **Main reframe: navigation is the *least* risky leg, not the most.** [Cutting the Cord](sources/cutting-the-cord-untethered-xlerobot.md) ran **RealSense D435 + RTAB-Map (localization-only) + [Nav2](entities/nav2.md)** on an *Orin Nano Super* — weaker than this robot's Orin NX 16 GB — in a $1,202 XLeRobot build. The fleet page independently says the framework "adds nothing" to navigation. The hard parts are **demonstration quality** and **the serial-bus conflict**.
- **The blocker that actually stands between "nav works" + "pick works" and "nav-then-pick works":** one serial port has exactly one owner, so a ROS 2 `JointState` publisher and LeRobot cannot both hold the FeeTech arm bus. Interim fix is time-slicing; the general fix is Rosetta owning the bus and serving both — which makes **Rosetta's arm-bus contract load-bearing for this project**, an obligation ladder step 1's one-line description hides.
- **Policy path settled by measured numbers, not preference.** On an Orin Nano: **ACT 36.0 ms / 27.8 Hz**, Diffusion Policy 539.6 ms / 1.8 Hz, **SmolVLA 713.8 ms / 1.4 Hz**. ACT is the only one that runs onboard at control rate → ACT first for a single task. Then [SmolVLA](entities/smolvla.md) **served off-board from the [Spark](entities/dgx-spark.md)** via LeRobot's async stack, which also preserves the 288 Wh runtime by keeping heavy inference off the robot. Cutting the Cord's diagnosis carried forward: the edge bottleneck is the **iterative action expert, not the VLM**, so SmolVLA's slowness is inherent to flow matching and unfixable by shrinking the language model.
- **Today's 5-DoF thread finally pays off practically — as a task-design rule, not an experiment.** SO-101 is fully dexterous **top-down** (roll becomes world yaw) and constrained **laterally** (radial approach only). So: design tasks around top-down grasps, and teleoperate a candidate grasp by hand before recording 50 demos of it. Also carried: reach ≈ 0.36 m and a 0.5–1.25 m vertical workspace with no lift, so **floor pickups are out**.
- **An advantage over [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md) worth recording:** XLeRobot's base runs FeeTech STS3215s with **absolute 0–4095 encoders**, so wheel odometry is available to fuse into RTAB-Map. Sourccey gave that up with open-loop PWM wheels — which is why its Oct-2026 "improved SLAM" roadmap item is harder than it reads.
- The non-holonomic consequence, already worked out in the fleet page and carried in: a differential base **cannot strafe**, so `navigate_to` must target a **grasp-ready pose head-on** rather than "get close, then align."
- Blocking decision recorded in the [backlog](backlog.md): **the depth camera**. D435i + the [existing bracket design](syntheses/projects/xlerobot-d435i-bracket.md) over the stock D415 — global shutter, wider FOV, and an IMU that materially helps RTAB-Map on a base that slips on carpet.

## [2026-08-13] update | D435i in hand — XLeRobot Phase 0 unblocked
- The depth-camera decision in the [bring-up plan](syntheses/projects/xlerobot-nav-manip-teleop-bringup.md) is settled: the **D435i is owned**, and it is the unit the [camera options analysis](syntheses/projects/xlerobot-camera-options-low-light.md) already recommended over the stock D415 — global shutter, wider FOV, onboard **IMU**. Close enough to [Cutting the Cord](sources/cutting-the-cord-untethered-xlerobot.md)'s D435 that its RTAB-Map + Nav2 recipe should transfer nearly directly.
- **Phase 0 changed from a decision into a half-day fit task.** The parametric L-bracket already exists at `hardware/xlerobot-d435i-bracket/` (`.stl` slice-ready 64×42×16 mm, `.scad` source), bolting to the camera's two front-face M3 holes at 45 mm pitch — needed because the stock press-fit shell is keyed to the slimmer D415 (99×20×23 mm vs D435i 90×25×25 mm).
- **Its two open parameters can now be resolved, because they required the hardware:** (1) **`cam_m3_z`**, the M3 hole height above the camera's bottom edge — **not published in Intel's D400 datasheet**, currently a 17 mm *estimate*, and the one most likely to bite; (2) the **robot-side bolt pattern**, still a placeholder in the `.scad`. Caliper both, re-export, print, test-fit, calibrate extrinsics.
- Phases 0, A1 (data-collection discipline) and B1 (RTAB-Map mapping run) can now all proceed in parallel. Recorded in the [backlog](backlog.md).

## [2026-08-13] lint | Full wiki pass — 940 pages
- Automated pass over **940 pages** (385 sources / 389 entities / 81 concepts / 79 syntheses). Punch list filed in [backlog](backlog.md); **nothing auto-fixed**, per the lint workflow.
- **The structural discipline is holding**: **0 broken internal links, 0 orphans, 0 duplicate titles, 0 date inconsistencies, 0 real Obsidian wikilinks** (3 hits are pages *documenting* the banned syntax), and **2 schema violations across 940 pages** (`libero-pro-paper` and `roboarena-paper` missing `ingested:`). Index coverage is **939/940**.
- **The one systemic defect: citations are one-directional.** **325 pairs** where an entity/concept cites a source page and that source page does **not** cite it back — across **172 entity/concept pages and 149 source pages**. The schema asks for both (`## Entities mentioned` on sources, `## Mentioned in` on entities); in practice the entity→source direction is maintained and the reverse rots, because an ingest adds entity links to *its own* source but never revisits older sources when a new entity page appears.
- **That also explains the `sources:` counts.** 322/470 exact, 129 understated, **19 overstated** — and the overstatements are *not* counting errors. `voyager` claims 3 sources with **0** inbound source links, yet its body cites 4 source pages, none of which link back. The counts are roughly right; **the back-links are missing.** Raised as a design question rather than a chore: should `sources:` be **derived** from inbound links at lint time instead of hand-maintained? A derived count cannot drift.
- **Two verified stale claims**, both wrong as written today: [LIBERO](entities/libero.md) still calls MolmoAct2 *"the wiki's highest LIBERO averages"* when [X-VLA](entities/x-vla.md) ties it at 98.1 and the [audit](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md) forbids ranking inside the tie; and [VLA deployability landscape](syntheses/platforms/vla-deployability-landscape.md) is still *titled* "the four axes" while the index records a fifth.
- **Closed a standing backlog item as a non-defect**: "four action-head families is now incomplete" — [llm-free-vla](concepts/learning/llm-free-vla.md) already positions itself as orthogonal, and the remaining "four families" hits are the world-model taxonomy, a different thing.
- **Biggest content gap: [Drake](entities/tri.md), mentioned in 16 pages with no page of its own** — the simulator/planner under TRI's whole LBM line, and present in UMI, Tedrake, DimOS manipulation, and xArm coverage. The wiki has the people and the products but not the tool. Next: **RDT/RDT-1B (12 pages)**, **Zenoh (12)**, **LangGraph (9)**, then the RoboTwin baseline cluster (DP3, BAKU, MemoryVLA, CogVLA, VLA-Adapter, FLOWER) — several of which sit *inside* the LIBERO tie the wiki reasons about.
- Also flagged: **10 pages marked `_stub_` but now >45 lines** (`genie-3`, `pi-zero-6`, `rt-2`, `agilex-piper`, `openvla`, …), and the nav/planning stack — **RTAB-Map, CuRobo, GTSAM** — now load-bearing for both project plans and uncovered.

## [2026-08-13] lint | Punch list worked — Drake / RDT / Zenoh ingested, `sources:` made derived
- Executed the [lint punch list](backlog.md). Everything mechanical is closed; the one judgment item is resolved by decision and the rest is now **reproducible**: `python3 scripts/lint_wiki.py` (`--fix-source-counts` to re-derive).
- **New: [`scripts/lint_wiki.py`](../scripts/lint_wiki.py)** — nine structural checks (schema, broken links, wikilinks, orphans, index coverage, source counts, one-way citations, dates, stale stubs). It **reports and does not gate**, per the wiki's own lint workflow; the single auto-fix is `--fix-source-counts`, which is mechanical and derivable. The one-off shell pass that produced this morning's punch list is now a committed artifact instead.
- **Closed the biggest content gap: [Drake](entities/drake.md)** (+ [source page](sources/drake-documentation.md)) — MIT CSAIL, core development led by [TRI](entities/tri.md), BSD-3, 4,148★, created 2014 and **still pushed daily**. Its thesis is a direct argument against black-box simulators: *"exposing the structure in the governing equations (sparsity, analytical gradients, polynomial structure, uncertainty quantification)"* so you can **optimize and verify**, not merely sample.
- **Two findings from the Drake ingest.** (1) [Tedrake](entities/russ-tedrake.md) authors Drake's canonical citation *and* leads TRI's [LBM](concepts/learning/large-behavior-models.md) program, and **TRI funds both daily** — institutional evidence that model-based and learned control are treated as complementary, not successive. Worth holding whenever this wiki tilts toward "learned policies replaced model-based control." (2) **Drake ships LCM first-class and marks ROS 2 "unsupported"** — the same choice [DimOS](entities/dimos.md) made independently this week. Recorded on [ROS 2](entities/ros2.md) as *middleware pluralism*: [Zenoh](entities/zenoh.md) is simultaneously a ROS 2 backend (`rmw_zenoh`) **and** a way not to use ROS 2, so discovery/transport/API are now separable choices rather than one incumbent.
- Also filed **[RDT-1B](entities/rdt.md)** — Tsinghua's 1.2 B diffusion bimanual foundation model, cited as a baseline in three ingested papers and never described. Its "Physically Interpretable Unified Action Space" is a **third** answer to cross-embodiment heterogeneity beside soft prompts and standardized collection; and it has the **smallest Easy→Hard drop of any policy in RoboTwin's table (−20.8)**, the cleanest instance of "pretraining buys robustness, not peak."
- **Decision recorded: `sources:` is now DERIVED** from inbound source-page links, regenerated by the script; **169 pages corrected**. It is undriftable but it is a **link-hygiene measure and a lower bound on provenance** — a source discussing an entity in prose without linking it does not count, so **`sources: 0` means "no ingested source links here," not "undocumented."** Fix the back-link, never the number.
- **That definition immediately paid off.** Seven entity pages now read `sources: 0`, including **[Tesla Optimus](entities/tesla-optimus.md)** — nothing ingested links to them, so their coverage is entirely secondhand from other entity pages. A provenance finding the hand-maintained counts had been hiding.
- **Repaired 9 unambiguous back-links** — cases where a source page named for an entity failed to link it (`voyager`, `saycan`, `dinov3`, `inner-monologue`, `language-to-rewards`, `ok-robot`, `rosetta`, `tri`, `smwm`).
- **Left open deliberately: ~306 one-way citations.** These are a **lead list, not a defect list** — the right question per pair is *"does this source actually discuss this entity?"*, and auto-adding all of them would turn source pages into link dumps. To be worked opportunistically whenever an ingest touches a source page.
- Final state: **schema 0 · broken links 0 · wikilinks 0 · orphans 0 · index coverage 100% · date inconsistencies 0 · source-count drift 0 · stale stubs 0.**

## [2026-08-13] ingest | Zenoh (properly) + the last of the §6 gaps
- **Zenoh had an entity page but no source page** — it was written secondhand from the [DimOS repo](sources/dimos-github.md). Now ingested from [zenoh.io](sources/zenoh-io.md) and the entity upgraded to primary.
- **The ingest closed its own open question.** The entity was filed with *"no measured numbers here — and for a robot control loop those are the only numbers that decide anything."* An **independent National Taiwan University benchmark** (published on Zenoh's blog with an arXiv companion) supplies them: across a real 100 Gb network, **Zenoh P2P 16 µs vs Cyclone DDS 37 µs, MQTT 45, Kafka 81**; throughput **>4M msg/s** vs DDS ~2M, Kafka 56–63K, MQTT 33–38K. Licence also confirmed: **EPL-2.0 / Apache-2.0**, not the `NOASSERTION` the GitHub API reports.
